# Draft 1.0 requirement diff against poppyseed 0.3

Primary source for every new or rewritten line is the snapshot in `spec/source/`, fetched 2026-11-14T00:37:44Z from the live Markdown pages. Those pages are draft 1.0, not a published release. Changelog quote, fetched the same way:

> ## Draft 1.0
> 
> In progress, at [/docs/spec](/docs/1.0/spec). This list grows until 1.0 is published.

Source URL: https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/changelog.md

Counts: 23 ids carried forward with the same level, role and text; 84 ids kept but edited; 472 ids added; 259 ids from 0.3 not present in draft 1.0.

A row is VERIFIED when the best-matching official sentence shares at least 55% of the requirement's content words (length > 3, stop-words removed). Otherwise the row is UNVERIFIED: the quote is the closest sentence, and a person should confirm it before treating the requirement as sourced.

| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.1 User IDs and Agent IDs | `sessions.agent-id-use` | MAY · company — The Company may use the Agent ID to tell a User's agents apart, for example in account settings or for rate limits, while they share the User's account, Account Tokens and conversations. | MAY · company — The Company may use the Agent ID to tell a User's agents apart, while they share the User's account, refresh tokens and conversations. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MAY use the Agent ID to tell a User's agents apart, but they share the User's account, refresh tokens, and conversations. | VERIFIED (0.92) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.1 User IDs and Agent IDs | `sessions.user-id-confidential` | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent keeps the mapping of each User's IDs and Agent IDs across Companies confidential, never publishing it or sharing it with third parties other than service providers acting for it. | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent keeps the mapping of each User's User IDs and Agent IDs across Companies confidential: it does not publish it or share it with third parties, other than service providers that process it on its behalf. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent holds the User IDs and Agent IDs of each of its Users at every Company, and MUST keep that mapping confidential: it MUST NOT publish it or share it with third parties, other than service providers that process it on the Personal Agent's behalf. | VERIFIED (0.82) |

| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.5 Ending Sessions and signing out | `sessions.remember-ended` | SHOULD · company — A Company remembers the keys of the Sessions it ended, for as long as it chooses, and rejects them with invalid_grant. | MAY · company — A Company may remember the sids of Sessions it ended, and a request for a remembered ended Session gets session_ended (HTTP 400). | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A request for a remembered ended Session gets session_ended (HTTP 400, JSON body), and the Personal Agent MUST then start a new Session with a new sid and send the request again in it. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.5 Ending Sessions and signing out | `signout.after` | MUST · company — After sign-out, what doesn't need the account carries on, and anything that used it, such as a conversation or an operation, stays as it is but answers a signed-out token with insufficient_scope. | MUST · company — After sign-out, what does not need the account carries on; anything that used the account stays as it is, but a request on it without an access token for that account gets a challenge, and a conversation stays open. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - What doesn't need the account carries on, such as a cart or a conversation that hasn't used it. - Anything that used the account, such as a conversation (8.11) or an operation ([Operations extension](/docs/1.0/extensions/operations#operation-object)), stays as it is, but a request on it without an access token for that account gets a challenge (section 7). | VERIFIED (0.78) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.join-before-browse` | MUST · agent — When a Company lists web.browser_session_endpoint, the Personal Agent joins its browser to a Session before browsing the Company's website. | MUST · agent — When the Company lists web.browser_session_endpoint, the Personal Agent joins its browser to a Session before browsing the Company's website; without the endpoint, it browses as an ordinary signed-out visitor. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Personal agents browse as ordinary signed-out visitors, and use your company agent and APIs for anything that needs the account. 3. **Signed-in browsing with the account's full access.** List browser_session_endpoint, set web.unscoped, and let poppy:browser-sign-in allow everything the account can do on the website. | VERIFIED (0.63) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.code` | MUST · company — The code has at least 128 random bits and expires no more than 60 seconds after it is issued. | MUST · company — The Company makes the browser-join code unguessable and short-lived, and each code works once. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - The form has a hidden field named code with a one-time code, and posts to the endpoint. - The Company MUST make the code unguessable and short-lived, and each code works once. - The Company MUST tie the code to the browser that loaded the page with a cookie set in the same response, so that only that browser can submit it. | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.join-cookie` | MUST · company — The Company ties the code to the browser that loaded the page with a cookie set in the same response, which is Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict and uses the __Host- prefix. | MUST · company — The Company ties the code to the browser that loaded the page with a cookie set in the same response; the cookie is not sent with a form submitted from another site, and other hosts on the Company's domain cannot set it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The cookie MUST NOT be sent with a form submitted from another site, and other hosts on the Company's domain MUST NOT be able to set it. | VERIFIED (0.56) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.cookie-lifetime` | MUST NOT · company — The cookie does not outlive the Session. | MUST NOT · company — The Session's cookie does not outlive the Session. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A browser joins a Session, signed in or out, through a code the Personal Agent authorizes, then carries the Company's cookie for it (section 6). | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.skip-unknown` | SHOULD · agent — Personal Agents skip API types they don't recognize. | SHOULD · agent — Personal Agents skip API types they do not recognize. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Acting at another Company uses that Company's discovery, Session, and any needed account authorization. - Personal Agents MUST skip reference types they don't recognize, so later versions can add types, such as the Company Agent or an extension. - An alternative is content from the Company (10.1). | VERIFIED (1.00) |

| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.mcp-extension-required` | MUST · company — An MCP server returns extension_required only to refuse the Personal Agent entirely, never for one tool. | MUST · company — An MCP server returns extension_required only to refuse the Personal Agent entirely, not for one tool. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | An MCP server returns it only to refuse the Personal Agent entirely, not for one tool. \| | VERIFIED (0.70) |
| spec/pap-1.0/09-tasks.md | 9.1 Excluded tasks | `tasks.not-offered` | MUST · company — A listed task isn't offered through any of the Company's interfaces: its Company Agent, its APIs, or its website in a browser session. | MUST · company — A listed task is not offered through the Company Agent, the APIs or the website in a browser session. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - A listed task isn't offered through any of the Company's interfaces: its Company Agent, its APIs, or its website in a browser session. - Whether the User's task is one listed is the Personal Agent's judgment, since each task is a phrase. - A limit that depends on the User's account, such as a transfer limit, isn't an excluded task. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/09-tasks.md | 9.2 Alternatives | `tasks.alternative` | MUST · company — An alternative has at least one of instructions, at most 2,000 characters, or references, each with a type and that type's fields. | MUST · company — An alternative has at least one of instructions or references. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | An alternative MUST have at least one of instructions or references. | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/09-tasks.md | 9.2 Alternatives | `tasks.ignore-unlisted` | MUST · agent — Personal Agents ignore an openapi or mcp reference that doesn't name a listed API, and skip reference types they don't recognize. | MUST · agent — Personal Agents ignore an openapi or mcp reference that does not name a listed API. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | From 1.0, a newer minor version won't break older Personal Agents, which use the document and ignore fields they don't recognize. \| \| supported_versions \| No \| The other protocol versions the Company serves, each mapped to the URL of that version's document (3.4). \| \| organization \| Yes \| The Company's display name, its domain, and an optional logo_uri, an HTTPS URL of the Company's logo | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/10-security.md | 10.2 Secrets and the model | `security.other-secrets` | SHOULD · agent — Personal Agents keep every other secret, such as one-time codes, out of model context, and sign and send requests in code the model can't read. | SHOULD · agent — Personal Agents keep every other secret out of model context too, such as one-time codes, and sign and send requests in code the model cannot read. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Personal Agents SHOULD keep every other secret out of model context too, such as one-time codes, and sign and send requests in code the model can't read. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.2 Advertising support | `ops.require-extension` | MUST NOT · company — A Company does not return operations to a Personal Agent that doesn't list the extension; it either performs the action as it would without the extension, or refuses with extension_required. | MUST NOT · company — A Company does not return operations in response to a request that does not declare operations version 3; it handles approval itself or refuses with extension_required and extension set to operations. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | It handles approval itself, such as on its website or in a Direct Conversation, or refuses the request with extension_required and extension set to operations ([spec 3.3](/docs/1.0/spec#extensions)). | VERIFIED (0.62) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.2 Advertising support | `ops.mcp-tool-error` | MUST · company — An MCP tool that needs the extension returns an ordinary tool error to a Personal Agent that doesn't list it, since an MCP server can't refuse a single tool call with extension_required. | MUST · company — An MCP tool that needs the extension returns an ordinary tool error to a Personal Agent that does not declare it, since an MCP server returns extension_required only to refuse the Personal Agent entirely. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | An MCP server returns that error only to refuse the Personal Agent entirely. | UNVERIFIED (0.47) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.3 The operation | `ops.complete-terms` | MUST · company — Everything that affects the action, such as the item, quantity, price, recipient, date or a condition for cancelling, is in the revision's summary, and in terms if present. | MUST · company — Everything that affects the action is in the revision's terms, and in summary if present. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | Everything that affects the action, such as what it changes, any amount, date, or recipient, or a condition for cancelling, MUST be in the revision's terms, and in summary if present. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.3 The operation | `ops.perform-confirmed-only` | MUST · company — The Company performs only what the confirmed revision describes. | MUST · company — The Company performs only what the approved revision describes. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | - **Approval covers the exact terms.** An approval covers one revision of the terms, and the Company performs only what that revision describes. - **At most once.** A retry with the same approval never performs the action twice (7). | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.3 The operation | `ops.no-early-start` | MUST NOT · company — The Company does not start the action before the operation is confirmed. | MUST NOT · company — The Company does not start the action before it receives an approval for it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | The Company MUST perform only what the approved revision describes, and MUST NOT start the action before it receives an approval for it. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.3 The operation | `ops.at-most-once` | MUST · company — The Company performs each operation at most once, however many times and through whichever channels it is confirmed. | MUST · company — Whichever revision is approved, the Company performs each operation at most once. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | Whichever revision is approved, the Company MUST perform each operation at most once (7). | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.5 Approving an operation | `ops.show-summary` | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent shows the User the Company's name and the revision's summary, and takes the User's answer through its own interface. | SHOULD · agent — When the operation has a summary, the Personal Agent shows it rather than write its own description. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | When the operation has a summary, the Personal Agent SHOULD show it rather than write its own description. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Sending the approval | `ops.message-not-confirmation` | MUST · both — A message doesn't confirm an operation, whatever it says; the Personal Agent confirms only at the operations endpoint. | MUST · both — In a conversation, the message's text does not approve anything; only the approval object does. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | It MUST NOT treat text from the Company, such as a conversation message, as the User's approval. - The Personal Agent can describe the terms itself, or show the values in terms. | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.8 Security considerations | `ops.revoke-false-approval` | MAY · company — A Company may revoke the client_id of a Personal Agent that confirms without the approval it claims. | MAY · company — A Company may revoke the client_id of a Personal Agent that sends an approval the User did not give or instruct. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | A Company MAY revoke the client_id of a Personal Agent that sends an approval the User didn't give or instruct ([spec 4.1](/docs/1.0/spec#agent-identity)). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.secrets` | SHOULD · both — Keep tokens, private keys, assertions, proofs, authorization codes, PKCE code verifiers, device codes, one-time codes, browser-session codes and mediated credentials out of logs, telemetry, error reports, model context, conversation messages and URLs, redacting the Authorization, DPoP and Cookie headers before logging. | SHOULD · both — Keep access tokens, refresh tokens, private keys, client assertions, Session JWTs, DPoP proofs, authorization codes, PKCE code verifiers, device codes, one-time codes, browser-session codes and mediated credentials out of logs, telemetry, error reports, model context, conversation messages and URLs, and redact the Authorization, Poppy-Session, DPoP and Cookie headers and credential fields before logging. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Access tokens and refresh tokens. - Private keys, client assertions, Session JWTs, and DPoP proofs. - Authorization codes, PKCE code verifiers, device codes, one-time sign-in codes, browser-session codes, and credentials sent in mediated sign-in. | UNVERIFIED (0.53) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.algorithms` | SHOULD · both — Sign assertions and DPoP Proofs with ES256, accept ES256 and RS256 in assertions, use RSA keys of at least 2048 bits, and reject alg: none and symmetric algorithms such as HS256. | SHOULD · both — Sign Session JWTs with ES256; sign client assertions and delegations with ES256 or, when signing blindly, PS384; accept both; use RSA keys of 2048 to 4096 bits; reject alg: none and symmetric algorithms. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Use RSA keys of 2048 to 4096 bits, and accept any RSA key in that range. - Reject alg: none and symmetric algorithms such as HS256. - Verify client assertions and delegations only with keys from the personal agent's verified jwks_uri ([4.1](/docs/1.0/spec#agent-identity)), and Session JWTs only with the key their verified delegation names in cnf.jwk ([5.2](/docs/1.0/spec#delegated-keys)). | VERIFIED (0.70) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.assertion-keys` | SHOULD · company — Verify assertions only with keys from the Personal Agent's verified jwks_uri, ignoring jku, x5u and any key embedded in an assertion. | SHOULD · company — Verify client assertions and delegations only with keys from the verified jwks_uri, and Session JWTs only with the key their verified delegation names, ignoring jku, x5u and any other embedded key. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Use RSA keys of 2048 to 4096 bits, and accept any RSA key in that range. - Reject alg: none and symmetric algorithms such as HS256. - Verify client assertions and delegations only with keys from the personal agent's verified jwks_uri ([4.1](/docs/1.0/spec#agent-identity)), and Session JWTs only with the key their verified delegation names in cnf.jwk ([5.2](/docs/1.0/spec#delegated-keys)). | VERIFIED (0.73) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.assertion-time` | SHOULD · company — Check aud, exp and iat, allow a small clock skew such as 30 seconds, and reject assertions that claim a long lifetime. | SHOULD · company — Check aud and iat, and exp on client assertions and delegations; accept Session JWTs only within the iat window; allow about 30 seconds of clock skew; reject assertions that claim a long lifetime. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Allow a small clock skew, such as 30 seconds, and reject assertions that claim a long lifetime. - Make the jti of each client assertion, delegation, Session JWT, and DPoP proof at least 128 random bits, and each Session ID too. - Personal agents: set a client assertion's exp at most five minutes after its iat. - Companies: reject a reused client assertion jti from the same client_id unti | VERIFIED (0.61) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.jti-entropy` | SHOULD · both — Make each jti at least 128 random bits. | SHOULD · both — Make each jti, and each Session ID, at least 128 random bits. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Allow a small clock skew, such as 30 seconds, and reject assertions that claim a long lifetime. - Make the jti of each client assertion, delegation, Session JWT, and DPoP proof at least 128 random bits, and each Session ID too. - Personal agents: set a client assertion's exp at most five minutes after its iat. - Companies: reject a reused client assertion jti from the same client_id unti | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.key-rotation` | SHOULD · agent — Give every key a kid, publish a new key before signing with it, and keep an old public key published until everything signed with it has expired. | SHOULD · both — Give every jwks_uri key a kid, publish a new key before signing with it, keep an old public key published until everything signed with it has expired, and do not rotate the DPoP key a refresh token was issued with. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Give every key in jwks_uri a kid. - Publish a new key in jwks_uri before signing with it, early enough for companies' caches to pick it up. - Keep an old public key published until every client assertion and delegation signed with it has expired. - Personal agents: rotate delegated keys as often as you like. | VERIFIED (0.68) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.fetch-limits` | SHOULD · both — Limit metadata fetches to a few redirects, a response size such as 100 KB, and a timeout of a few seconds, and validate HTTPS certificates. | SHOULD · both — Limit metadata fetches to a few redirects, a response size such as 100 KB and a timeout of a few seconds, and validate HTTPS certificates. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Set limits: a few redirects at most, a response size such as 100 KB, and a timeout of a few seconds. - Validate HTTPS certificates. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.idempotency-key` | SHOULD · both — Send an Idempotency-Key (a random UUID v4) on API writes that accept one, and as a Company keep idempotency records per session for a set time, returning the original response for a repeated key and rejecting a repeated key with a different body. | SHOULD · both — Send an Idempotency-Key (a random UUID v4) on API writes that accept one, and as a Company keep idempotency records per client_id, User ID and account for a set time, returning the original response for a repeated key and rejecting a repeated key with a different body. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | The company may reject a jti it has already seen. - Wait at least as long as Retry-After says on a 429 (MUST, [5.6](/docs/1.0/spec#sending-requests)) or a 503. - Otherwise, back off exponentially with jitter, cap the delay, and limit the number of attempts. - Companies keep idempotency records for a set time, such as a day, scoped to the personal agent's client_id and the user ID, and to the | UNVERIFIED (0.38) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.retries` | SHOULD · both — Each retry needs a new DPoP Proof and a retried token request needs a new assertion; wait at least Retry-After on 429 or 503, and otherwise back off exponentially with jitter. | SHOULD · both — Each retry needs a new Session JWT, a new DPoP proof when the token is DPoP-bound, and a new assertion for a token request; wait at least Retry-After on 429 or 503, and otherwise back off exponentially with jitter. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Use a random UUID (version 4), and reuse the same key when you retry the same request. - Give each conversation request, such as a message or a handoff request, an id with at least 128 random bits, and reuse it when you retry the request ([8.3](/docs/1.0/spec#sending-messages)). - Each retry needs a new Session JWT, and a new DPoP proof if the access token is DPoP-bound. | UNVERIFIED (0.53) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.rate-limits` | SHOULD · company — Limit by client_id, by User and by Session, counting a request against a client_id only after its client assertion's signature checks pass, and send the RateLimit and RateLimit-Policy headers. | SHOULD · company — Limit by client_id, by User and by Session, counting a request against a client_id only after its Session JWT or client assertion's signature checks pass, and send the RateLimit and RateLimit-Policy headers. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Count a request against a client_id only after its Session JWT or client assertion's signature checks pass. | UNVERIFIED (0.53) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.agent-rate-limits` | SHOULD · agent — Track each Company's limits across tasks and Users, prefer long polling or streaming over short polls, and spread work that isn't time-sensitive outside busy hours. | SHOULD · agent — Track each Company's limits across tasks and Users, prefer long polling or streaming over short polls, and spread work that is not time-sensitive outside busy hours. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Limit unverified requests by network instead. - Companies: also send the RateLimit and RateLimit-Policy headers ([IETF draft](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers/)), so personal agents can slow down before they hit a limit. - Personal agents: keep track of limits for each company and share them across your tasks and users, rather than letting each task find th | VERIFIED (0.62) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.discovery` | SHOULD · agent — When the Company reached is on a different domain from the one the User asked for, tell the User which Company it is before signing in or acting for them. | SHOULD · agent — When the Company reached is on a different domain from the one the User asked for, tell the User the organization.name and domain before signing in or acting, and show that the name is unconfirmed; show logo_uri next to the domain, never in place of it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | When the company you reach is on a different domain from the one the user asked for, tell the user which company you're dealing with, its organization.name and domain, before you sign in or act for them. | UNVERIFIED (0.47) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.user-ids` | SHOULD · agent — Make User IDs random per User and Company, or derive them with a keyed hash of a random internal ID and the Company's full auth.issuer, and tell Users that Companies recognize them across signed-out visits. | SHOULD · agent — Make User IDs random per User and Company, or derive them with a keyed hash of a random internal ID and the Company's identifier, key them by the full issuer or the issuerless HTTPS domain, and tell Users that Companies recognize them across signed-out visits. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | This is the simplest choice. - **Derive one with a keyed hash**, such as HMAC-SHA256 of your internal user ID and the company's identifier (its issuer, or its HTTPS domain without one), with a secret key. | UNVERIFIED (0.45) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.anonymous` | SHOULD · agent — For anonymous browsing, create a random User ID and Session Key for each browsing session, discard both when it ends, and never sign in with them. | SHOULD · agent — For anonymous browsing, create a random User ID for each browsing session, give it its own sid and delegated keys, discard it when the session ends, and never send an access token with it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST NOT send an access token in a Session that uses an anonymous browsing User ID (5.1). | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.token-storage` | SHOULD · agent — Encrypt Account Tokens at rest away from the model, keep Session Tokens in memory, key tokens by the Company's full auth.issuer, exchange for a Bearer token only for an MCP server without DPoP, and delete an Account Token on invalid_grant. | SHOULD · agent — Encrypt refresh tokens at rest away from the model, keep access tokens in memory, key them by the Company's full issuer, request a resource-specific token when protected-resource metadata requires one, and on invalid_grant continue signed out. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Keep the client private key and DPoP keys in a key service or hardware security module that signs without exporting them, and store refresh tokens apart from them. - Store refresh tokens by the company's full issuer, as for [user IDs](#agent-user-ids), so a token from one company hosted by a provider is never sent to another. - Before the first signed-in call to a service, fetch its protected reso | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.sign-in-choice` | SHOULD · agent — Prefer Direct or Device Sign-In to Mediated Sign-In, and Direct to Device, read each scope's description, use auth.scope_hints to decide which scopes need the User's go-ahead (treating a scope without hints as irreversible with personal data), ask for the fewest scopes the task needs, and check scope in the response. | SHOULD · agent — Prefer Direct or Device Sign-In to Mediated Sign-In, and Direct to Device; use descriptions and auth.scope_hints, treating a scope without hints as irreversible with personal data; ask for the fewest scopes the task needs; and do not send the User to sign in again for a scope the response omitted. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.direct-device` | SHOULD · agent — Open the authorization URL in the User's own browser, make state random and single-use, send the URL to the User's phone before falling back to Device Sign-In, show verification_uri_complete with user_code, poll no faster than interval, and add 5 seconds on slow_down. | SHOULD · agent — Open the authorization URL in the User's own browser, make state random and single-use, use PKCE with S256, use PAR when the endpoint is listed, and for Device Sign-In show verification_uri_complete with user_code and poll no faster than interval. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Never type the user's password, enter their one-time code, or click the company's approval controls for them. - Open the authorization URL in the user's own browser, not in a browser you automate. - Make state random and single-use. | UNVERIFIED (0.41) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.agent-mediated` | SHOULD · agent — Collect mediated fields in a form the model never sees, show the Company's name and domain on it, and don't sign in again with stored credentials after the User disconnects the Personal Agent. | SHOULD · agent — Collect mediated fields in a form the model never sees, look up stored credentials by the issuer, and do not sign in again with stored credentials after the User disconnects the Personal Agent. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Collect the extension's fields in a form that sends them straight to your sign-in code, so secret values never pass through the model. - Look up stored credentials by the company's issuer, and show the company's name and domain on the form and when asking for a code (MUST, [Mediated Sign-In 6](/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in#security)). | VERIFIED (0.61) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.distributed-apps` | SHOULD · agent — Keep the signing private key on a server, not in a distributed app, keep Session Keys in the device's platform keystore, and return to a phone app through an HTTPS link it claims or Device Sign-In, and to a desktop app through a loopback redirect URI. | SHOULD · agent — Keep the signing private key on a server, keep delegated keys and DPoP keys in the device's platform keystore with the refresh token they belong to, and return to a phone app through an HTTPS link it claims or Device Sign-In. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | The app generates one in its platform keystore and asks your server for a delegation for it, then signs Session JWTs itself ([5.2](/docs/1.0/spec#delegated-keys)). [10.7](/docs/1.0/spec#delegated-key-security) explains what this hides from your own servers, and blind signing, which hides more. - Keep each DPoP key with the refresh token it was issued with, on the device or on your server, since yo | UNVERIFIED (0.52) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.browsing` | SHOULD · agent — Use a separate browser storage context per Session, never type the User's credentials into a website, join right before browsing, read the browser-session code only from the endpoint's page and fill the form with page access rather than the model, and close the context when the Session ends. | SHOULD · agent — Do not browse a Company that sets web.disabled; use a separate browser storage context per Session; never type the User's credentials into a website; read the browser-session code only from the endpoint's page; and close the context when the Session ends. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Sign in with direct, device, or mediated sign-in, then join your browser to the session ([section 6](/docs/1.0/spec#web-browsing)). - Join the browser to the session right before you start browsing, and join again after signing in, or when the website stops recognizing the session. - A join without an access token leaves a login the browser already has, while a signed-in join replaces it, and the  | UNVERIFIED (0.38) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.agent-conversations` | SHOULD · agent — Read events with a long wait or a stream, summarize before a handoff, write for a person who takes over, and close conversations when the task is done. | SHOULD · agent — Read until working is false, use as_of_request and as_of_cursor, show disclosures and link domains, withdraw a handoff that is no longer needed, and close a Direct Conversation before continuing in the parent. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Leave as_of_request off messages from people. - If your help desk can withdraw a request for a person, do so on a handoff cancel, so no one is assigned to a conversation that no longer needs them ([8.9](/docs/1.0/spec#handoff)). - Keep a conversation's events for at least 30 days, so a personal agent that reconnects rarely hits cursor_expired ([8.5](/docs/1.0/spec#reading-events)). - On a stre | UNVERIFIED (0.43) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.interfaces` | SHOULD · agent — Prefer a Company's APIs and Company Agent over browsing, don't phone a Company that publishes a poppy.json, and skip API types and conversation protocols you don't recognize. | SHOULD · agent — Prefer a Company's APIs and Company Agent over browsing, do not phone a Company that publishes a poppy.json, and skip API types and conversation protocols you do not recognize. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Prefer the company's APIs and company agent over browsing its website. | UNVERIFIED (0.40) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.excluded-tasks` | SHOULD · agent — Read the excluded tasks before trying a task, tell the User right away when it is one, offer the alternative, and try an alternative from feedback before saying the task can't be done. | SHOULD · agent — Get the tasks response early in each Session, tell the User when a task is excluded, offer the alternative, and try an alternative from feedback before saying the task cannot be done. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | A company's APIs may also have feedback operations meant for other clients. - When a company answers your feedback report with an alternative, try it before you tell the user the task can't be done ([9.3](/docs/1.0/spec#feedback)). - When you skip a company's skill index, or a skill in it, because you don't recognize its $schema or type, say so to the user if it matters for the task, so they k | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.scope-descriptions` | SHOULD · company — Describe each scope in words a User understands, specific enough for a model to match tasks to it, saying whether it only reads and whether a write scope also allows reading, and which of its actions can't be undone and what personal data it shows. | SHOULD · company — Describe each scope so it says what it unlocks, which tasks need it, and whether the task can be done without it, and give auth.scope_hints only when you are sure. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A description is most useful when it says what the scope unlocks, which tasks need it, and whether a task can be completed without it, such as a scope that only personalizes search results. | VERIFIED (0.56) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.consent-page` | SHOULD · company — On the consent page, show the Personal Agent's name, logo and client_id domain, the account, the scopes in plain language with the new ones marked, and how long access lasts; protect it against CSRF and framing; accept only PKCE S256; and return iss in every redirect. | SHOULD · company — Name the Personal Agent as wanting to connect, show its client_id domain, list every scope including ones already granted, show the page for prompt=consent and for every Device Sign-In, accept PKCE S256 only, and return iss on every redirect. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Without prompt=consent, you can skip the page for scopes the user already approved, as your server does for other clients. - Protect the page against cross-site request forgery, and against framing by other sites with Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors. - redirect_uri MUST exactly match an entry in the personal agent's redirect_uris ([4.2](/docs/1.0/spec#direct-sign-in)). - Accept PK | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.device` | SHOULD · company — Assess the risk of Device Sign-In and choose mitigations, ask the User to confirm the code matches, return slow_down to fast pollers, limit wrong user_code entries, and keep expires_in short, such as 10 minutes. | SHOULD · company — Offer Direct Sign-In as well, follow RFC 10027 §2, put the verification page on the issuer's host or the Company's domain, and keep expires_in short. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | A company whose accounts hold them, such as a bank, may offer only direct sign-in. - Follow the best practices for cross-device flows in [RFC 10027](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc10027) §2 (MUST, [4.3](/docs/1.0/spec#device-sign-in)): assess the risk before offering device sign-in, and choose mitigations from its §6.1, such as short-lived single-use codes, rate limits, limited scopes, and a co | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.company-mediated` | SHOULD · company — Plan the removal of Mediated Sign-In; until then rate-limit it by account, client_id and User ID, cap wrong codes, require a code on the first sign-in after a disconnect, return the same failure for an unknown account and a wrong password, never store raw credentials, and tell the User when a Personal Agent signs in. | SHOULD · company — Offer Mediated Sign-In only until Direct or Device Sign-In can replace it; rate-limit attempts, require a one-time code when a sign-in looks unusual, and never store raw credentials. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Rate-limit attempts by account, by client_id, and by network address, and cap wrong one-time codes. - Require a one-time code when a sign-in looks unusual, including a personal agent's first mediated sign-in after the user disconnected it. - Return the same invalid_grant error for an unknown account and a wrong password, so responses don't reveal whether an account exists. - Never store raw  | VERIFIED (0.68) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.account-settings` | SHOULD · company — List each connected Personal Agent with its name, domain, scopes and last use, with a control to disconnect it. | SHOULD · company — List each connected Personal Agent and offer a control that disconnects it by revoking its refresh tokens. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - In account settings, list each connected personal agent with its name, domain, scopes, and when it was last used. - Offer a control to disconnect each one. | UNVERIFIED (0.55) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.scopes-everywhere` | SHOULD · company — Apply the same scope rules on the website, APIs, Company Agent and live agents through one shared check, and check read and write scopes separately. | SHOULD · company — Apply the same scope checks on the website, APIs, Company Agent and live agents, and show a visible error that names missing scopes. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Apply the same scope rules on your website, APIs, company agent, and live agents. | VERIFIED (0.56) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.hosting` | SHOULD · company — A provider hosting several Companies publishes metadata for each issuer at its own URL, serves each storefront's poppy.json on its own domain, records the issuer with every Session and Account Token, and keeps each Company's records apart. | SHOULD · company — Give each hosted Company its own issuer, publish that issuer's metadata and each storefront's poppy.json on the storefront's domain, and check the issuer on every request. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | VERIFIED (0.69) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.company-conversations` | SHOULD · company — Keep a conversation's events for at least 30 days, send an SSE comment every 15 to 30 seconds on an idle stream, leave conversations that used the account open after sign-out answering signed-out tokens with insufficient_scope, and say so before closing an inactive conversation. | SHOULD · company — Report working from acceptance until the reply is finished, set as_of_request on Company Agent messages, keep events at least 30 days, and ask for sign-in with an authorization event rather than verification questions in messages. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | In a direct conversation, reply as you would to the customer on your own channels. - Report working as true from the moment you accept a request until you've acted on it and finished replying, so a personal agent that reads until working is false gets the whole reply ([8.7](/docs/1.0/spec#state)). - Set as_of_request on each company agent message to the latest request the agent saw when  | UNVERIFIED (0.45) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.company-excluded-tasks` | SHOULD · company — List the tasks you chose not to offer the way a customer would ask for them, give an alternative whenever there is one, set caching headers on the document, and answer a feedback report for a task you do offer with an alternative. | SHOULD · company — List tasks you chose not to offer, give an alternative when there is one, and when feedback asks for a task you do offer, answer with an alternative. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | A personal agent rejects a file whose digest doesn't match the index it got. - When a feedback report asks for a task you do offer, answer with an alternative that says how, so the personal agent can finish the task ([9.3](/docs/1.0/spec#feedback)). - Take feedback from personal agents at the feedback.endpoint in your tasks response ([9.3](/docs/1.0/spec#feedback)). | VERIFIED (0.64) |

| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3 Discovery | `discovery.skill-urls` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Skill artifact URL references are resolved before the HTTPS rule applies, and the resolved URL is what that rule covers. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Skill artifact URL references (9.4) are resolved before this rule applies. | VERIFIED (0.64) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.1 Fields | `discovery.nonstandard-port` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — A Personal Agent uses a Company on a port other than 443 only when configured to on purpose. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Personal Agents use a Company on another port only when configured to on purpose, such as for testing (section 3). | VERIFIED (0.63) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.1 Fields | `discovery.logo-https` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — organization.logo_uri, when present, is an HTTPS URL. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | From 1.0, a newer minor version won't break older Personal Agents, which use the document and ignore fields they don't recognize. \| \| supported_versions \| No \| The other protocol versions the Company serves, each mapped to the URL of that version's document (3.4). \| \| organization \| Yes \| The Company's display name, its domain, and an optional logo_uri, an HTTPS URL of the Company's logo | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.1 Fields | `discovery.issuer-choice` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — When protected-resource metadata lists more than one authorization server, auth.issuer is present and is one of them; with one authorization server the Company may leave auth.issuer out, and if it includes it, it names that server. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Domains are compared ignoring case, with no other normalization. \| \| auth \| No \| The Company's sign-in details: issuer, disabled, scopes, and scope_hints, below. \| \| auth.issuer \| If the domain's protected resource metadata lists more than one authorization server \| The Company's OAuth issuer identifier, naming the one Personal Agents use (3.2). | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.1 Fields | `discovery.auth-disabled` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — auth.disabled may be true to offer Personal Agents no sign-in even though an issuer exists, and it defaults to false. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | With one authorization server, the Company MAY leave it out, and if it includes it, it MUST name that server. \| \| auth.disabled \| No \| true if the Company offers Personal Agents no sign-in, even though it has an issuer (3.2). | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.1 Fields | `discovery.protocol-entry` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Each agent.protocols entry has a type, an HTTPS endpoint and a description of what the agent there can help with. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | They apply wherever the scope is described. \| \| agent \| At least one of agent, apis, or web \| The Company Agent. protocols lists its supported conversation protocols, each with a type, an HTTPS endpoint, and a description of what the agent there can help with (section 8). | VERIFIED (0.90) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.1 Fields | `discovery.protocol-access` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A poppy conversation entry may include an access hint of session or signed_in. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A poppy entry may include an access hint (3.2). \| \| apis \| At least one of agent, apis, or web \| A list of APIs. | VERIFIED (0.56) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.1 Fields | `discovery.api-entry` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Each API entry has a type, a url and a short description. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Each has a type, such as those in section 7, a url, and a short description that helps the Personal Agent pick the right one. \| \| web \| At least one of agent, apis, or web \| The Company's website. | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.1 Fields | `discovery.browser-endpoint` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — web.browser_session_endpoint may name where the Personal Agent's browser joins a Session. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The optional browser_session_endpoint is where the Personal Agent's browser joins a Session (section 6). | VERIFIED (0.78) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.1 Fields | `discovery.web-disabled` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — web.disabled may be true to ask Personal Agents not to browse the website in a browser, and it defaults to false. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Without it, Personal Agents browse the site as ordinary signed-out visitors, unless web.disabled is set. \| \| web.disabled \| No \| true if the Company asks Personal Agents not to browse its website in a browser, and to use its Company Agent and APIs instead (section 6). | VERIFIED (0.80) |

| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.2 OAuth server | `issuer.sign-in-requires-issuer` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A Company that offers sign-in has an OAuth issuer. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company that offers sign-in MUST have an OAuth issuer. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.2 OAuth server | `issuer.signed-out-without-oauth` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company without an issuer may offer signed-out Sessions, including APIs, conversations, feedback and browser join, without an authorization server. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company without one can offer signed-out Sessions, including APIs, conversations, feedback, and browser join, without running an authorization server. | VERIFIED (0.93) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.2 OAuth server | `issuer.identifier` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — The Company's identifier is its issuer when it has one, and otherwise https://{organization.domain} with the host in lowercase and no trailing slash; Session JWTs use it as their audience, and User IDs and Agent IDs are scoped by it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company's identifier is its issuer when it has one, and otherwise https://{organization.domain}, with the host in lowercase and no trailing slash. | VERIFIED (0.61) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.2 OAuth server | `issuer.resource-metadata` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A Company with an issuer publishes protected-resource metadata for https://{organization.domain} at https://{organization.domain}/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource, and its resource is exactly https://{organization.domain}. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent finds the issuer from the protected resource metadata ([RFC 9728](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728)) of https://{organization.domain}, which the Company publishes at https://{organization.domain}/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource when it has an issuer. | VERIFIED (0.92) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.2 OAuth server | `issuer.no-issuer-signal` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — HTTP 404, or valid metadata with authorization_servers absent or empty, means the Company has no issuer, and that is valid only when auth.issuer is absent and the Company offers no sign-in. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - HTTP 404, or valid metadata with authorization_servers absent or empty, means the Company has no issuer. | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.2 OAuth server | `issuer.no-fallback` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — A Personal Agent does not use the domain fallback after a timeout, another error status, invalid metadata or a failure to verify an issuer, and does not fall back for a Company whose issuer it already knows unless a successful discovery explicitly establishes that the issuer was removed. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | This is valid only when auth.issuer is absent and the Company offers no sign-in. - A Personal Agent MUST NOT use the domain fallback after a timeout, another error status, invalid metadata, or a failure to verify an issuer. | VERIFIED (0.58) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.2 OAuth server | `issuer.single-server` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — If protected-resource metadata lists one authorization server, that server is the Company's issuer. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Domains are compared ignoring case, with no other normalization. \| \| auth \| No \| The Company's sign-in details: issuer, disabled, scopes, and scope_hints, below. \| \| auth.issuer \| If the domain's protected resource metadata lists more than one authorization server \| The Company's OAuth issuer identifier, naming the one Personal Agents use (3.2). | VERIFIED (0.89) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.2 OAuth server | `issuer.other-resources` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — protected_resources may list other resources, such as the Company's MCP servers. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It MAY list other resources too, such as the Company's MCP servers. | VERIFIED (0.83) |

| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.2 OAuth server | `issuer.resource-scopes` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — Protected-resource metadata may list scopes_supported instead of or as well as the issuer's metadata. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The protected resource metadata can also list the scopes a Personal Agent can request in scopes_supported ([RFC 9728](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728) §2), instead of or as well as the issuer's metadata (below, 4.7). | VERIFIED (0.90) |

| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.2 OAuth server | `discovery.access-not-permission` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — An access hint grants no permission; runtime challenges and authorization events remain authoritative. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The hint grants no permission: runtime challenges and authorization events remain authoritative. | VERIFIED (0.82) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.3 Extensions | `extensions.declare` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A Personal Agent declares the extensions it uses with a Company, one version of each, in the Poppy-Extensions header, and does not need to declare an extension the Company recognizes from the request itself. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Each extension defines the entry's other fields for each of its versions. - A Personal Agent declares the extensions it uses with a Company, one version of each, in the Poppy-Extensions header (3.4). | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.3 Extensions | `extensions.schema` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — An extension that defines conversation-message content publishes a JSON Schema for that content. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It doesn't need to declare an extension that the Company recognizes from the request itself, such as one that defines its own grant type. - Personal Agents and Companies MUST ignore extensions and versions they don't support and fields they don't recognize, so either side can work with the other under this specification alone. - An extension can define content for conversation messages, which they | UNVERIFIED (0.44) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.same-host` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Each URL in supported_versions is an HTTPS URL on the host that served poppy.json, after any redirects. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - Each URL in supported_versions MUST be an HTTPS URL on the host that served poppy.json, after any redirects. | VERIFIED (0.90) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.no-foreign-document` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — A Personal Agent does not use a supported_versions document whose URL is on another host, and does not follow a redirect from it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST NOT use a document whose URL is on another host, and MUST NOT follow a redirect from it. | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.listed-version` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — A Personal Agent does not use a linked document whose protocol_version is not the version it is listed under, and that document has no supported_versions of its own. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The document there has the protocol_version it is listed under, and no supported_versions. | VERIFIED (0.64) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.same-company` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Every document a Company serves at the same time has the same organization.domain and selects the same issuer. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Its path can be anything, unlike poppy.json's (section 3), because poppy.json names it. - Every document a Company serves at the same time MUST have the same organization.domain and select the same issuer (3.2), so that a new version doesn't change the Company's identifier, and with it User IDs and refresh tokens. | VERIFIED (0.90) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.other-fields` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — Other fields may differ by version. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | From 1.0, a newer minor version won't break older Personal Agents, which use the document and ignore fields they don't recognize. \| \| supported_versions \| No \| The other protocol versions the Company serves, each mapped to the URL of that version's document (3.4). \| \| organization \| Yes \| The Company's display name, its domain, and an optional logo_uri, an HTTPS URL of the Company's logo | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.choose` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — The Personal Agent chooses the newest version, among protocol_version and supported_versions, that is compatible with one it implements, and does not use any of them if none is; two versions are compatible when they have the same major version and, before 1.0, the same minor version. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent chooses the newest version, among protocol_version and the versions in supported_versions, that is compatible with one it implements, and uses that version's document. | VERIFIED (0.69) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.header` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — On every request with the Session header, the Personal Agent sends Poppy-Version as major.minor, a version it implements that is compatible with the chosen one. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Declaring versions.** On every request with the Session header (5.2), the Personal Agent sends the protocol version it implements, compatible with the chosen one, in Poppy-Version, as major.minor. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.extensions-header` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — When it uses extensions, it also sends Poppy-Extensions as an RFC 9651 List of Strings, each an extension name from the chosen document with the version it uses in the v parameter. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | When it uses extensions with the Company, it also sends Poppy-Extensions: an [RFC 9651](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9651) List of Strings, each the name of an extension in the chosen document, with the version it uses as a String in the v parameter. | VERIFIED (0.93) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.no-extensions-header` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — Without Poppy-Extensions, or when it fails to parse, the request declares no extensions. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Without Poppy-Extensions, or when it fails to parse (RFC 9651 §4.2), the request declares no extensions. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.stable-session` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent sends the same Poppy-Version and Poppy-Extensions on every request in a Session, and starts a new Session to change them. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent can have several Sessions for the same User (section 5). \| \| Session header \| The Poppy-Session header on every request a Personal Agent sends to a Company's APIs and endpoints: a JWT the Personal Agent signs for that request, naming the Session and the User ID (5.2). \| \| Delegated key \| A short-lived key a Personal Agent signs its Session JWTs with. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.check` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — An endpoint that verifies the Session header also checks Poppy-Version, and rejects a missing header or a version that is not compatible with one the Company serves with version_unsupported (HTTP 400, JSON body). | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It rejects a request without it, or with a version that isn't compatible with one the Company serves, with version_unsupported (HTTP 400, JSON body), and MUST NOT handle the request under another version. | VERIFIED (0.56) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.no-other-version` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — That endpoint does not handle the rejected request under another version. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It rejects a request without it, or with a version that isn't compatible with one the Company serves, with version_unsupported (HTTP 400, JSON body), and MUST NOT handle the request under another version. | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.retry` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — After version_unsupported, the Personal Agent fetches poppy.json again without using a cached copy and chooses again; if it chooses another version, it starts a new Session and sends the request again, once, and otherwise it stops sending the request. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent fetches poppy.json again, and if it chooses another version, starts a new Session and sends the request again, once. \| \| extension_required \| 403 \| In a JSON body. | VERIFIED (0.61) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.sunset` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company that plans to stop serving a version may say so with the Deprecation and Sunset headers. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company that plans to stop serving a version MAY say so in its responses to requests in that version, with the Deprecation ([RFC 9745](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9745)) and Sunset ([RFC 8594](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8594)) headers. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/03-discovery.md | 3.4 Versions | `versions.stable-meaning` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — From 1.0, the location of poppy.json and how it is fetched, protocol_version, supported_versions, Poppy-Version and version_unsupported keep their meaning in every version. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | From 1.0, the location of poppy.json and how it is fetched (section 3), protocol_version, supported_versions, Poppy-Version, and version_unsupported keep their meaning in every version, so a Personal Agent and a Company that implement different major versions can find one they share, or tell that there is none. | VERIFIED (0.92) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4 OAuth | `oauth.prefer-direct` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — A Company that offers sign-in offers Direct Sign-In. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It also says which of Direct and Device Sign-In the Company offers (4): authorization_code for Direct Sign-In and urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:device_code for Device Sign-In. - authorization_endpoint starts Direct Sign-In (4.2). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4 OAuth | `oauth.agent-prefer-direct` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent uses Direct Sign-In when the Company offers it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent SHOULD use Direct Sign-In when the Company offers it. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4 OAuth | `oauth.refuse-client` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may turn off sign-in for every Personal Agent, or refuse sign-in or one sign-in type by client_id. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company can turn off sign-in for every Personal Agent (3.2), or refuse sign-in, or one sign-in type, by client_id (4.1). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.1 Agent identity | `identity.jwks` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — jwks_uri is an HTTPS URL on the same domain as client_id, and the key set contains only public keys. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - client_id MUST equal the URL the document was fetched from. - jwks_uri MUST be an HTTPS URL on the same domain as client_id. | VERIFIED (0.56) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.1 Agent identity | `identity.redirect-uris` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — Each redirect_uris entry is an HTTPS URL on the same domain as client_id, or a loopback redirect URI as RFC 8252 §7.3 defines. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Each entry MUST be an HTTPS URL on the same domain as client_id, or a loopback redirect URI for an app on the User's computer, as [RFC 8252](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8252) §7.3 defines. - The Personal Agent authenticates at the token endpoint with a client assertion signed by a key from jwks_uri (private_key_jwt, [RFC 7523](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523)). | VERIFIED (0.82) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.1 Agent identity | `identity.client-assertion-fresh` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A client assertion is short-lived and includes iat and a unique, unguessable jti. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST make them short-lived, and MUST include iat and a unique, unguessable jti. | VERIFIED (0.63) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.1 Agent identity | `identity.algorithms` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Companies accept client assertions signed with ES256 and with PS384. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MAY reject a reused jti, as RFC 7523 §3 allows. - Companies MUST accept client assertions signed with ES256 and with PS384. PS384 lets a Personal Agent have its client assertions signed blindly (10.7). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.1 Agent identity | `identity.refused` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A refused Personal Agent gets invalid_client at the authorization server, or invalid_session at a resource. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A refused Personal Agent gets the OAuth invalid_client error at the authorization server, or invalid_session at a resource (5.3). | VERIFIED (0.91) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.1 Agent identity | `identity.unauthorized-client` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may accept a Personal Agent for signed-out Sessions but refuse it sign-in, or one sign-in type, with unauthorized_client. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company can also accept a Personal Agent for signed-out Sessions but refuse it sign-in, or one sign-in type, with the OAuth unauthorized_client error from the grant's endpoint. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.1 Agent identity | `identity.choose-companies` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · agent — A Personal Agent decides which Companies it uses the protocol with, and the requirements on Personal Agents apply when it does. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Likewise, a Personal Agent decides which Companies it uses the protocol with. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.check-iss` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent checks that iss exactly matches the issuer of the Company it started sign-in with. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST check that iss exactly matches the issuer of the Company it started sign-in with. | VERIFIED (0.89) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.client-assertion` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The token request carries the Personal Agent's client assertion. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | This stops a response from another Company's sign-in being swapped in. - The token request carries the Personal Agent's client assertion (4.1). - Unless the Personal Agent receives the redirect only on the device that opened the authorization URL, as below, the authorization request MUST include prompt=consent ([OpenID Connect Core](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#AuthReque | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.prompt-consent` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Unless the Personal Agent receives the redirect only on the device that opened the authorization URL, the authorization request includes prompt=consent. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | This stops a response from another Company's sign-in being swapped in. - The token request carries the Personal Agent's client assertion (4.1). - Unless the Personal Agent receives the redirect only on the device that opened the authorization URL, as below, the authorization request MUST include prompt=consent ([OpenID Connect Core](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#AuthReque | VERIFIED (0.92) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.support-prompt` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — The Company supports prompt=consent, whether or not it supports OpenID Connect, and shows its consent page for a request that includes it, even when the account already granted every requested scope to the client_id. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company SHOULD support prompt=consent, whether or not it supports OpenID Connect, and show its consent page for a request that includes it, even when the account already granted every requested scope to the client_id (10.8). | VERIFIED (0.95) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.ignore-unknown-prompt` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company that does not support prompt=consent ignores it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company SHOULD support prompt=consent, whether or not it supports OpenID Connect, and show its consent page for a request that includes it, even when the account already granted every requested scope to the client_id (10.8). | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.require-par` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may require Pushed Authorization Requests by setting require_pushed_authorization_requests to true. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Pushed Authorization Requests.** A Company MAY require Pushed Authorization Requests (PAR, [RFC 9126](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126)) by setting require_pushed_authorization_requests to true in its issuer's metadata (3.2). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.use-par` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — When the Company requires PAR, the Personal Agent sends the authorization parameters to pushed_authorization_request_endpoint with its client assertion, and opens authorization_endpoint with only client_id and request_uri. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent then MUST send the authorization request's parameters to the pushed_authorization_request_endpoint with its client assertion, and open the authorization_endpoint with only client_id and the request_uri it gets back. | VERIFIED (0.69) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.prefer-par` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent uses PAR whenever the metadata lists pushed_authorization_request_endpoint. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company that offers sign-in SHOULD list every scope a Personal Agent can request here, in scopes_supported of its protected resource metadata (above), or in both (4.7). - token_endpoint_auth_signing_alg_values_supported lists the signing algorithms the token endpoint accepts for client assertions, as RFC 8414 §2 defines (4.1). | VERIFIED (0.60) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.par-dpop` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A Personal Agent that sends DPoP proofs sends one with the pushed request, so the Company can bind the code to the key. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent that sends DPoP proofs (4.5) sends one with the pushed request too, so the Company can bind the code to the key ([RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) §10.1). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.par-link` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — When the Personal Agent sends the User a link to open later, the link goes to a page of its own that pushes the request when the User opens it and then redirects to authorization_endpoint. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | When the Personal Agent sends the User a link to open later, such as on their phone, it SHOULD link to a page of its own that pushes the request when the User opens it and then redirects to the authorization_endpoint. | VERIFIED (0.93) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.cross-device` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — Companies that offer Direct Sign-In, and Personal Agents that open the authorization URL on a device other than the one the User is using, follow the best practices in RFC 10027 §2. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Companies that offer Direct Sign-In, and Personal Agents that open the authorization URL on a device other than the one the User is using, MUST follow the best practices in [RFC 10027](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc10027) (BCP 247) §2, as for Device Sign-In (4.3). | VERIFIED (0.94) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.2 Direct Sign-In | `direct.same-device` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent receives the redirect only on the device that opened the authorization URL when it can. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | This stops a response from another Company's sign-in being swapped in. - The token request carries the Personal Agent's client assertion (4.1). - Unless the Personal Agent receives the redirect only on the device that opened the authorization URL, as below, the authorization request MUST include prompt=consent ([OpenID Connect Core](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#AuthReque | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.3 Device Sign-In | `device.cross-device` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — Companies that offer Device Sign-In and Personal Agents that use it follow the best practices in RFC 10027 §2, including its guidance on when to use the device authorization grant. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Companies that offer Direct Sign-In, and Personal Agents that open the authorization URL on a device other than the one the User is using, MUST follow the best practices in [RFC 10027](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc10027) (BCP 247) §2, as for Device Sign-In (4.3). | VERIFIED (0.73) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.3 Device Sign-In | `device.show-link` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Before showing verification_uri and user_code, or verification_uri_complete, the Personal Agent checks that the link's host is the host of the Company's issuer or organization.domain, or a subdomain of either, and shows the Company's organization.name and domain with them. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Before showing them, the Personal Agent MUST check that the link's host is the host of the Company's issuer or organization.domain (3.1), or a subdomain of either, and MUST show the Company's organization.name and domain with them. | VERIFIED (0.68) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.3 Device Sign-In | `device.keep-code-private` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent keeps device_code private. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Following [draft-ietf-wimse-aims](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-wimse-aims/) §8, which keeps an agent's credentials out of the model's reach, Personal Agents MUST keep these out of model context: access tokens (4.5), refresh tokens (4.4), Session JWTs (5.2), private keys, authorization codes and PKCE code verifiers (4.2), and device codes (4.3). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.3 Device Sign-In | `device.client-assertion` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Each poll carries the client assertion. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | This stops a response from another Company's sign-in being swapped in. - The token request carries the Personal Agent's client assertion (4.1). - Unless the Personal Agent receives the redirect only on the device that opened the authorization URL, as below, the authorization request MUST include prompt=consent ([OpenID Connect Core](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#AuthReque | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.4 Refresh tokens | `refresh.downscope` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · agent — A refresh request may send scope to ask for fewer scopes than the refresh token has, and resource for a token for one service. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It MAY send scope to ask for fewer scopes than the refresh token has, for example read-only access for a task that only looks things up, and resource for a token for one service (4.5). | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.4 Refresh tokens | `refresh.rotation` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent uses a new refresh token if the response has one, and keeps the old one otherwise. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - Refresh token rotation follows RFC 6749 §6: the Personal Agent uses a new refresh token if the response has one, and keeps the old one otherwise. - A refresh token isn't tied to a Session. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
