| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.4 Refresh tokens | `refresh.not-a-session` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — A refresh token is not tied to a Session, and any of the User's Sessions can use access tokens from it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Any of the User's Sessions can use access tokens from it (5.4). - A copied refresh token is useless without a client assertion from the Personal Agent, and without the DPoP key when the Company binds it to one (below). | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.4 Refresh tokens | `refresh.same-dpop-key` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A Personal Agent that sends a DPoP proof when it gets a refresh token sends a proof from the same key with every refresh of that refresh token and of the refresh tokens that replace it, and keeps the key unless the User signs in again or an extension the Company requires defines how to replace it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **DPoP keys at refresh.** A Personal Agent that sends a DPoP proof when it gets a refresh token (4.5) MUST send a proof from the same key with every refresh of that refresh token, and of the refresh tokens that replace it. | VERIFIED (0.57) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.4 Refresh tokens | `refresh.bind-dpop` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may bind a refresh token to the key of the DPoP proof in the request that issued it, and the refresh tokens that replace it, and then rejects a refresh whose proof is missing or from another key with invalid_grant and issues nothing bound to that key, except through such an extension's replacement procedure. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It then rejects a refresh whose proof is missing or from another key with invalid_grant, and issues nothing bound to that key, except through such an extension's replacement procedure. [RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) §5 leaves a confidential client's refresh tokens unbound, because client authentication already limits who can use them. | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.5 Access tokens | `tokens.authorization-scheme` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A signed-in request carries an access token in the Authorization header with the scheme the token response's token_type names. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A signed-in request carries an access token in the Authorization header, with the scheme the token response's token_type names, as [RFC 6750](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750) or, for DPoP-bound tokens, [RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) describes. | VERIFIED (0.92) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.5 Access tokens | `tokens.support-dpop` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — Personal Agents support DPoP. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Personal Agents SHOULD support DPoP, because a Personal Agent that doesn't can't sign in at a Company that requires it. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.5 Access tokens | `tokens.send-dpop` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A Personal Agent that supports DPoP sends a DPoP proof with its token requests when the issuer's metadata lists dpop_signing_alg_values_supported. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent that supports it sends a DPoP proof with its token requests when the issuer's metadata lists dpop_signing_alg_values_supported ([RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) §5.1), so the Company can bind the tokens. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.5 Access tokens | `tokens.dpop-one-user` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent uses each DPoP key for one User ID only, and keeps it for every refresh of the refresh tokens it got with it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST use each DPoP key for one User ID only, for the same reason as delegated keys (5.2), and keeps it for every refresh of the refresh tokens it got with it (4.4). | VERIFIED (0.89) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.5 Access tokens | `tokens.bearer-choice` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — The Company decides for each token whether to bind it, and returns token_type Bearer for one it does not bind. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company decides for each token whether to bind it, and returns token_type Bearer for one it doesn't bind, as RFC 9449 §5 allows, such as a token for a service that doesn't accept DPoP. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.5 Access tokens | `tokens.session-ath` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A signed-in request also carries the Session header, whose ath covers the access token. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A signed-in request also carries the Session header (5.2), whose ath covers the access token. | VERIFIED (0.90) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.5 Access tokens | `tokens.resource-metadata` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Before its first signed-in call to an API or a conversation endpoint, the Personal Agent fetches that service's protected-resource metadata and uses it only if resource is the URL it derived the metadata URL from. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - Before its first signed-in call to an API or a conversation endpoint, the Personal Agent fetches the metadata at the URL RFC 9728 §3.1 defines from the service's URL (an MCP server's url, the URL in an OpenAPI description's servers, or the endpoint). | VERIFIED (0.73) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.5 Access tokens | `tokens.resource-parameter` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — If that metadata exists and its resource is not https://{organization.domain}, the Personal Agent requests a token with resource set to it and uses that token only there. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | If the metadata exists and its resource isn't https://{organization.domain}, the Personal Agent requests a token with resource set to it ([RFC 8707](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707)), at sign-in or refresh, and uses that token only there. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.5 Access tokens | `tokens.resource-only-there` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company accepts a token issued for a resource only at that service, and returns invalid_target for a resource it does not serve. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Otherwise the default token works there, as it does at the other endpoints this protocol defines. - The Company accepts a token issued for a resource only at that service, and returns invalid_target for a resource it doesn't serve. - A service that gets a token not issued for it returns invalid_token with resource_metadata in its challenge (RFC 9728 §5.1), so a Personal Agent with stale  | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.5 Access tokens | `tokens.wrong-resource` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A service that gets a token not issued for it returns invalid_token with resource_metadata in its challenge. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Otherwise the default token works there, as it does at the other endpoints this protocol defines. - The Company accepts a token issued for a resource only at that service, and returns invalid_target for a resource it doesn't serve. - A service that gets a token not issued for it returns invalid_token with resource_metadata in its challenge (RFC 9728 §5.1), so a Personal Agent with stale  | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.6 Revocation | `revoke.refresh` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent revokes a refresh token at revocation_endpoint with token_type_hint=refresh_token and its client assertion. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent revokes a refresh token at the Company's revocation_endpoint ([RFC 7009](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7009)), with token_type_hint=refresh_token and its client assertion. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.6 Revocation | `revoke.sign-out` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Revoking every refresh token it holds for an account signs out. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Sign-out.** The Personal Agent signs out of an account by revoking every refresh token it holds for it (4.6). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.browser-sign-in` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — The protocol defines one scope, poppy:browser-sign-in, which lets the Personal Agent sign its browser in to the Company's website; each Company defines its other scopes. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The protocol defines one, poppy:browser-sign-in, which lets the Personal Agent sign its browser in to the Company's website (section 6). | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.extra-sources` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · agent — The Personal Agent may also request scopes documented by the Company's APIs or sign-in extensions, even when absent from both scopes_supported lists. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It MAY also request scopes documented by the Company's APIs or sign-in extensions, even when absent from both. | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.hidden` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — Either scopes_supported list may leave out scopes the Company offers only to its other clients, and the Company may limit the scopes it grants by client. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Either list MAY leave out scopes the Company offers only to its other clients, and the Company MAY limit the scopes it grants by client, as [RFC 6749](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.3) §3.3 allows. | VERIFIED (0.92) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.describe` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — The Company describes each scope it offers Personal Agents, in an OpenAPI OAuth security scheme it lists or otherwise in auth.scopes, and when both describe a scope the Personal Agent uses auth.scopes. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - The Company SHOULD describe each scope it offers Personal Agents, so the Personal Agent can choose the ones a task needs: in the scopes of an OAuth 2.0 security scheme in an OpenAPI description it lists (section 7), or otherwise in auth.scopes. | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.hints` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may give hints about any scope in auth.scope_hints, with changes of none, reversible or irreversible and personal_data true or false, describing the most consequential action that requires the scope. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - changes is none if no action changes the account or anything else, reversible if every change can be undone, such as editing a saved address, and irreversible if any change can't be undone, such as cancelling an order, deleting data, sending a message, or paying. - personal_data is true if any action shows or changes the User's personal data, such as contact details, addresses, or pa | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.hint-fields` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A Company includes both changes and personal_data in each auth.scope_hints entry. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Recommended for scopes no OpenAPI description the Company lists describes, and used over an OpenAPI description of the same scope (4.7). \| \| auth.scope_hints \| No \| Hints about what each scope allows, keyed by scope name: changes and personal_data (4.7). | VERIFIED (0.70) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.unknown-hints` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A Personal Agent ignores hint fields it does not recognize, and treats a changes value it does not recognize as irreversible. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent MUST ignore fields it doesn't recognize, and treat a changes value it doesn't recognize as irreversible, so later versions can add both. | VERIFIED (0.70) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.no-extra` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — The Company does not grant scopes beyond those requested. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - The Company MUST NOT grant scopes beyond those requested. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.request-scope` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Each sign-in request includes scope, except where an extension continues an earlier request, as a space-separated string. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - Each sign-in request MUST include scope, except where an extension continues an earlier request, as a space-separated string, following [OAuth scope syntax](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.3). | VERIFIED (0.85) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.invalid-scope` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Missing or unknown scopes are rejected with invalid_scope. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Missing or unknown scopes are rejected with invalid_scope. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.fewer` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — The Company may grant fewer scopes than requested, and the response's scope says which it granted. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MAY grant fewer scopes than requested, for any reason RFC 6749 §3.3 allows, and the response's scope says which it granted. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.least` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent requests only the scopes the User's tasks need. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - A Personal Agent SHOULD request only the scopes the User's tasks need, following [RFC 9700](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9700#section-2.3) §2.3. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.reauthorize-all` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — If a task needs more access than the refresh token allows, the Personal Agent requests every scope it still needs, including previously granted scopes it wants to keep, and does not request only the missing scopes. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - If a task needs more access than the refresh token allows, the User signs in again, and the Personal Agent requests every scope it still needs, including previously granted scopes it wants to keep, following [RFC 6749 §3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.3). | VERIFIED (0.90) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.revoke-replaced` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — The Personal Agent revokes the refresh token that the new one replaces, and revoking a replaced refresh token is not sign-out. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | \| Poppy term \| OAuth term \| Why the Poppy name \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| User ID \| Pairwise subject identifier, sub in the Session JWT \| The Personal Agent assigns it, not the Company's OAuth server as in OpenID Connect (5.1). \| \| Sign-in \| User authorization \| It names the User's approval of the Personal Agent's access, whatever the flow (section 4). \| \| Sign-out \| Revoking the refresh token \| It | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.pending-unchanged` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — A pending or failed sign-in leaves existing tokens unchanged. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A pending or failed sign-in leaves existing tokens unchanged. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.refresh-rotation` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — Refresh may return a replacement refresh token. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Refresh (4.4) returns an access token and MAY return a replacement refresh token, as RFC 6749 §6 describes. | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.check-response` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent checks scope before continuing; the response leaves scope out only when the Company granted exactly the scopes requested. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The User may have approved fewer scopes than requested, so the Personal Agent checks scope before continuing. | VERIFIED (0.62) |
| spec/pap-1.0/04-oauth.md | 4.7 Scopes and token responses | `scopes.at-least-one` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A Poppy sign-in grants at least one scope; if the User approves none, it ends as a decline. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Poppy sign-in grants at least one scope: if the User approves none, it ends as a decline. | VERIFIED (0.91) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 1.1 Protocol layers | `layers.independent` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — Sessions and account access are independent: a signed-out request on an API or protocol endpoint carries the Session header, and a signed-in request also carries an access token. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | On APIs and protocol endpoints, a signed-out request carries the Session header, and a signed-in request also carries an access token. | VERIFIED (0.62) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 1.1 Protocol layers | `layers.verify-before-use` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Relying on Session identity, ownership, continuity or state requires verification, and an endpoint that uses no Session claims can handle requests without verifying the header. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | If the endpoint uses no Session claims, it can serve requests without verifying the header ([5.3](/docs/1.0/spec#session-verification)). - **Session continuity.** Verify Session JWTs when you want to recognize a pseudonymous user, maintain a Session cart, or connect activity across channels. | VERIFIED (0.59) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 1.1 Protocol layers | `layers.send-anyway` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent sends the Session header even when the Company ignores it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent still sends the header even when the Company ignores it. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 1.1 Protocol layers | `layers.operator-not-session` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — Operator recognition alone does not verify Session claims, authorize account access, or decide whether the Company accepts the request. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Operator recognition alone does not verify Session claims, authorize account access, or decide whether the Company accepts the request. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5 Sessions | `sessions.first-request` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — There is no separate step to start a Session: the first request with a new sid starts it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | There's no separate step to start a Session: the first request with a new sid starts it. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5 Sessions | `sessions.signed-in-per-request` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — A Session is signed out unless the request carries an access token. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | If the access token is also DPoP-bound (4.5), the request carries both the Session JWT and a DPoP proof, each signed with its own key. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.1 User IDs and Agent IDs | `sessions.anonymous` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · agent — A Personal Agent may give a User a new User ID for each anonymous browsing session, uses that ID for nothing else, and discards it when the browsing session ends. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent uses the ID for nothing else, discards it when the browsing session ends, and MUST NOT send an access token in a Session that uses it (5.4). | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.header-required` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent sends a Poppy-Session header on every request to a Company's APIs, conversations and other resource endpoints this protocol or an extension defines, using destinations from poppy.json. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST send a Poppy-Session header on every request to a Company's APIs, conversations, and other resource endpoints this protocol or an extension defines. | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.header-elsewhere` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — The Personal Agent does not send a Company's Session JWT to any other destination, except the website pages this section allows, and discovery, description documents and OAuth endpoints do not carry the header. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST NOT send a Company's Session JWT to any other destination, except the website pages below. | UNVERIFIED (0.53) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.version-with-header` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Requests that carry the Session header also carry Poppy-Version, and Poppy-Extensions when the Personal Agent uses extensions with the Company. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Requests that carry it also carry Poppy-Version, and Poppy-Extensions when the Personal Agent uses extensions with the Company (3.4). | VERIFIED (0.82) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.typ` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — The Session JWT's typ is poppy-session+jwt. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Both carry the Poppy Session header as above. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.alg` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — The Session JWT's alg is ES256, it is signed with a delegated key, kid is that key's JWK thumbprint, and poppy_dlg carries its delegation. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The JWT is signed with a delegated key, kid is that key's JWK thumbprint, and poppy_dlg carries its delegation (Delegated keys, below). - iss is the Personal Agent's client_id (4.1). - aud is the Company's identifier (3.2), as a single string (10.4). | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.claims` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — iss is the Personal Agent's client_id, aud is the Company's identifier as a single string, sub is the User ID, htm and htu are the request's method and URL without the query or fragment, iat is when it was signed, and jti is unique and unguessable. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It uses the same characters as Conversation IDs (8.2). - sub is the User ID for this Company (5.1). - agent_id is the optional Agent ID (5.1). - htm and htu are the request's method and URL, without the query or fragment, as in a DPoP proof ([RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) §4.2). - iat is when the Personal Agent signed it, in Unix seconds, and jti is unique and ungue | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.sid` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent chooses sid at random so it cannot be guessed, and does not use it for any other Session. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST choose it at random, so it can't be guessed, and MUST NOT use it for any other Session, so Companies can't link Sessions by it. | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.ath` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — ath is the base64url SHA-256 hash of the access token when the request carries one, and is left out otherwise. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It uses the same characters as Conversation IDs (8.2). - sub is the User ID for this Company (5.1). - agent_id is the optional Agent ID (5.1). - htm and htu are the request's method and URL, without the query or fragment, as in a DPoP proof ([RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) §4.2). - iat is when the Personal Agent signed it, in Unix seconds, and jti is unique and ungue | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.required-claims` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent includes every claim except agent_id, and except ath when there is no access token. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST include every claim except agent_id, and ath when there's no access token. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.same-subject` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent sends the same sub and agent_id, or no agent_id, in every Session JWT for a Session. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent can have several Sessions for the same User (section 5). \| \| Session header \| The Poppy-Session header on every request a Personal Agent sends to a Company's APIs and endpoints: a JWT the Personal Agent signs for that request, naming the Session and the User ID (5.2). \| \| Delegated key \| A short-lived key a Personal Agent signs its Session JWTs with. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.page-fetch` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent that fetches a page of the Company's website with its own HTTP client, rather than a browser, sends the Session header, only for HTTPS URLs whose host is exactly organization.domain. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Website pages.** A Personal Agent that fetches a page of the Company's website with its own HTTP client, rather than a browser, SHOULD send the Session header with it, so the Company can tell which Personal Agent and Session the request is from. | VERIFIED (0.60) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.page-no-token` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — The Personal Agent does not send an access token with those page fetches, so the request is signed out. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST NOT send an access token with these requests, so the JWT has no ath and the request is signed out (5.4), even when a browser joined to the Session is signed in. | VERIFIED (0.78) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.page-redirect` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — When a page redirects, the Personal Agent signs a new Session JWT for the new URL only if its host is still organization.domain, and otherwise follows the redirect without the header. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | When a page redirects, the Personal Agent signs a new Session JWT for the new URL only if its host is still organization.domain, and otherwise follows the redirect without the header. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `session.page-retry-without` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · agent — After a page that verifies the header rejects a failed check with invalid_session, the Personal Agent may fetch the page again without the header. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A page that verifies the header rejects a failed check with invalid_session, as any endpoint does (5.3), so the Personal Agent learns of a mistake in its header. | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.algorithms` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Companies accept delegations signed with ES256 and with PS384. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MAY reject a reused jti, as RFC 7523 §3 allows. - Companies MUST accept client assertions signed with ES256 and with PS384. PS384 lets a Personal Agent have its client assertions signed blindly (10.7). | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.typ` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — A delegation's typ is poppy-delegation+jwt. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It is signed with a delegated key, which a key in the Personal Agent's jwks_uri vouches for in a delegation carried in the JWT's poppy_dlg header (5.2). - **Interactions above OAuth:** browser join, with its poppy:browser-sign-in scope (section 6), APIs (section 7), conversations (section 8), excluded tasks, skills, and feedback (section 9), and extensions (3.3), including [Operations](/docs | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.no-private` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — cnf.jwk does not contain private key members. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It MUST NOT contain private key members. - iat and exp are when the delegation was issued and when it expires. exp MUST be at most 24 hours after iat. | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.lifetime` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — A delegation's exp is at most 24 hours after iat. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It MUST NOT contain private key members. - iat and exp are when the delegation was issued and when it expires. exp MUST be at most 24 hours after iat. | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.no-user` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — A Personal Agent does not put a User ID, an Agent ID, a Company's issuer, or anything else about the User or the Companies they use in a delegation. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent chooses the lifetime within that limit. - jti is a unique, unguessable ID for the delegation. - The Personal Agent MUST NOT put a User ID, an Agent ID, a Company's issuer, or anything else about the User or the Companies they use in a delegation. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.no-late-sign` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — The Personal Agent does not sign a Session JWT after its delegation's exp. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - poppy_dlg is the delegation, in compact form. - kid is the delegated key's JWK thumbprint ([RFC 7638](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7638)). - The Personal Agent MUST NOT sign a Session JWT after its delegation's exp. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.reject` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — When verifying a Session header, the Company rejects, with invalid_session, a Session JWT without a delegation, including one signed directly with a key from jwks_uri, and a Session JWT whose delegation fails the delegation checks. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | When verifying a Session header (5.3), the Company MUST reject, with invalid_session, a Session JWT without a delegation, including one signed directly with a key from jwks_uri, and a Session JWT whose delegation fails any of these checks. | VERIFIED (0.93) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.cache` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A resource may cache a verified delegation until it expires. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A resource sees many Session JWTs with the same delegation, so it MAY cache a verified delegation until it expires. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.key-not-stable` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — A Company does not expect a User's delegated key to stay the same between requests or Sessions, and does not treat a new one as a reason to reject a request. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | So a Company MUST NOT expect a User's delegated key to stay the same between requests or Sessions, or treat a new one as a reason to reject a request. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.one-user` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent uses each delegated key, and its delegation, for one User ID only. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST use each delegated key, and its delegation, for one User ID only: it signs with it only that User ID's Session JWTs. | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.2 The Session header | `delegation.reject-second-user` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may reject, with invalid_session, a Session JWT whose delegated key has already signed for a different User ID while a delegation for the key lasts. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company MAY reject, with invalid_session, a Session JWT whose delegated key has already signed for a different User ID while a delegation for the key lasts. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.verify` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Companies verify the Session header on every request that carries it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Companies SHOULD verify the Session header on every request that carries it. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.verify-before-use` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company completes the Session checks before relying on Session claims for client or User identity, ownership, continuity, or access to state, including creating or continuing a Session. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MUST complete the following checks before relying on Session claims for client or User identity, ownership, continuity, or access to state, including creating or continuing a Session: | VERIFIED (0.94) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.fetch-client` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Verification fetches the client metadata from iss without following redirects and the public keys from its jwks_uri, following the address checks in section 3. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Fetches the client metadata from iss without following redirects, as the CIMD draft (§5) requires, and the public keys from its jwks_uri (4.1), following the address checks in section 3. 2. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.check-claims` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Verification checks typ, that aud is the Company's identifier compared character for character, that htm and htu match the request, and that iat is within an acceptance window the Company chooses; the JWT never grants account access. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Verifies the JWT's delegation and signature, as Delegated keys (5.2) describes, then typ and the claims above: aud is its Company's identifier, compared character for character; htm and htu match the request as RFC 9449 §4.3 describes for DPoP proofs; and iat is within an acceptance window it chooses, such as one minute. | VERIFIED (0.63) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.check-ath` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — If the request carries an access token, the Company checks that ath matches it and that the token was issued to the same client_id as iss; without an access token, ath is absent. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | If the request carries an access token, checks that ath matches it, and that the token was issued to the same client_id as iss. | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.lookup` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company looks up the Session by client_id, sid and User ID; an unknown combination starts a signed-out Session, and a known one continues it, including the ended-Session check. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | An unknown combination starts a signed-out Session with the verified client_id, User ID, and optional Agent ID, and a known one continues its Session. | VERIFIED (0.64) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.skip-unused` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — An endpoint that uses no Session claims may handle a request without verifying the header or requiring one. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | An endpoint that uses no Session claims MAY handle a request without verifying the header or requiring one. | VERIFIED (0.91) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.no-unverified-use` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — The Company does not use unverified Session claims to attribute a request to a Personal Agent, User or Session, or to look up their state. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MUST NOT use unverified Session claims to attribute a request to a Personal Agent, User, or Session, or to look up their state. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.errors` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A failed Session check returns HTTP 400 with {"error": "invalid_session"}, and an ended Session returns {"error": "session_ended"}; either may include error_description. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A failed Session check returns HTTP 400 with {"error": "invalid_session"}, and an ended Session returns {"error": "session_ended"} (5.5). | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.missing` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company rejects a request without the Session header when handling it depends on Session claims. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MUST reject a request without the Session header when handling it depends on Session claims. | VERIFIED (0.89) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.replay` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may reject a reused Session JWT jti. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MAY reject a reused jti, as RFC 7523 §3 allows. - Companies MUST accept client assertions signed with ES256 and with PS384. PS384 lets a Personal Agent have its client assertions signed blindly (10.7). | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.operator-signature` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent also signs requests that carry the Session header with an HTTP Message Signature using Web Bot Auth. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent SHOULD also sign requests carrying the Session header with an HTTP Message Signature ([RFC 9421](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9421)), using [Web Bot Auth](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-webbotauth-httpsig-protocol/), so traffic-management infrastructure can authenticate the operator before the request reaches the Company's application. | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.operator-covers` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The operator signature covers poppy-session, and authorization when present. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent's operator signature MUST cover poppy-session, and authorization when present, so it binds to the Session JWT and access token. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.operator-not-session` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — An operator signature's absence does not make a Session JWT invalid, and verifying it does not replace verifying the Session JWT. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Its absence does not make a Session JWT invalid. | UNVERIFIED (0.44) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.3 Verifying the Session header | `session.operator-independent` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Before trusting any Session claim, the Company verifies the Session JWT independently. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Before trusting any Session claim, the Company MUST verify the Session JWT independently (5.3). | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.4 Signing in | `signin.anonymous-token` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — The Personal Agent does not send an access token in a Session that uses an anonymous browsing User ID. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST NOT send an access token in a Session that uses an anonymous browsing User ID (5.1). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.4 Signing in | `signin.reject-account-change` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may reject a request whose access token is for a different account than its Session used before, with session_ended. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company MAY reject a request whose access token is for a different account than its Session used before, with session_ended (5.5). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.4 Signing in | `signin.new-session-for-account` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent starts a new Session rather than use a second account in one Session. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent SHOULD start a new Session rather than use a second account in one Session, so accounts don't get mixed up across Sessions. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.4 Signing in | `signin.challenge` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A request without an access token is signed out, so an account action without one gets a challenge, and an access token without the needed scopes gets insufficient_scope. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A request without an access token is signed out, so an account action without one gets a challenge, and an access token without the needed scopes gets insufficient_scope (section 7). | VERIFIED (0.93) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.5 Ending Sessions and signing out | `sessions.lifetime` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company decides how long a Session lasts and keeps its record only that long. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company decides how long a Session lasts, and keeps its record, which 5.3 finds by client_id, sid, and User ID, only that long. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.5 Ending Sessions and signing out | `sessions.session-ended-retry` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — After session_ended, the Personal Agent starts a new Session with a new sid and sends the request again in it; if the new Session gets session_ended too, it stops sending the request. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | The Personal Agent handles them as section 7 of the specification says: for session_ended it starts a new Session and sends the request again, once, and for invalid_session it stops sending the request until it fixes the cause. | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.5 Ending Sessions and signing out | `signout.revoke-all` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent signs out of an account by revoking every refresh token it holds for it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Sign-out.** The Personal Agent signs out of an account by revoking every refresh token it holds for it (4.6). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.5 Ending Sessions and signing out | `signout.sessions-continue` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — The Sessions that used those refresh tokens continue signed out: the Personal Agent sends the Session header without an access token. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Sessions that used them continue signed out: the Personal Agent sends the Session header without an access token. | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.5 Ending Sessions and signing out | `signout.learn` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent finds out the User disconnected it when a request fails with invalid_token or refresh returns invalid_grant, and then continues signed out. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent finds out when a request fails with invalid_token, or refresh returns invalid_grant, and then continues signed out. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.5 Ending Sessions and signing out | `signout.no-resignin` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD NOT · agent — The Personal Agent does not sign in to that account again unless the User asks. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A conversation stays open, so the User can sign in again and continue it. - The Personal Agent SHOULD NOT sign in to that account again unless the User asks. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.6 Sending requests | `requests.no-cookie` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Companies accept the Session header and access token at their APIs, conversations and other protocol and extension endpoints without requiring cookies. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - Companies MUST accept the Session header and access token at their APIs, conversations, and other protocol and extension endpoints without requiring cookies. | VERIFIED (0.94) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.6 Sending requests | `requests.no-impersonate` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — Personal Agents do not leave out the Session header to appear as a person, including on website pages they fetch without a browser, other than fetching a page again after invalid_session. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A browser on the website carries a cookie instead (section 6). - Personal Agents MUST NOT leave out the Session header to appear to the Company as a person on their own, including on website pages they fetch without a browser, other than fetching a page again after invalid_session (5.2). | VERIFIED (0.95) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.6 Sending requests | `requests.not-in-url` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · both — Personal Agents do not send tokens or Session JWTs in a URL, and Companies do not accept them from one. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A browser follows section 6. - Personal Agents MUST NOT send tokens or Session JWTs in a URL, and Companies MUST NOT accept them from one. | VERIFIED (0.89) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.6 Sending requests | `requests.rate-limit` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A Company that limits request rate returns HTTP 429 with a Retry-After header when a request goes over the limit. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Rate limits.** A Company that limits how often a Personal Agent can make requests returns HTTP 429 ([RFC 6585 §4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6585#section-4)) with a Retry-After header ([RFC 9110 §10.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-10.2.3)) when a request goes over the limit, on any of its endpoints, APIs, conversations, or website. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/05-sessions.md | 5.6 Sending requests | `requests.retry-after` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent waits at least as long as Retry-After says before sending that request again. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST wait at least that long before sending the request again. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.disabled` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD NOT · agent — A Personal Agent does not use a browser on the website of a Company that sets web.disabled. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent SHOULD NOT use a browser on the website of a Company that sets it, and uses the interfaces the Company lists instead. web.disabled doesn't cover pages the Personal Agent fetches without a browser (5.2). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.disabled-shape` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — A Company that sets web.disabled does not list browser_session_endpoint, and lists agent or apis. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company that sets web.disabled MUST NOT list browser_session_endpoint, and MUST list agent or apis. | VERIFIED (0.90) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.own-browser` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent joins only a browser it controls to a Session, never the User's own browser. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST join only a browser it controls to a Session, never the User's own browser. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.page-isolation` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — The browser-join page is not stored by caches, framed by other pages, or kept as a window reference by the page that opened it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company chooses its name and value. - The page MUST NOT be stored by caches, framed by other pages, or kept as a window reference by the page that opened it. - Loading the page MUST NOT start, end, or change any Session, or change the browser's other cookies. | VERIFIED (0.92) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.page-no-session` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — Loading the browser-join page does not start, end or change any Session, or change the browser's other cookies. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company chooses its name and value. - The page MUST NOT be stored by caches, framed by other pages, or kept as a window reference by the page that opened it. - Loading the page MUST NOT start, end, or change any Session, or change the browser's other cookies. | VERIFIED (0.90) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.authorize-checks` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The authorize request checks the Session header and the access token if any, checks that the code was issued, has not expired and has not been authorized before, and then records it for that Session. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | UNVERIFIED (0.43) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.authorize-error` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A code that fails those checks gets HTTP 400 with {"error": "invalid_request"}. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A code that fails these checks gets HTTP 400 with {"error": "invalid_request"}. | VERIFIED (0.89) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.browser-scope` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company rejects an access token in the authorize step that does not have poppy:browser-sign-in, with insufficient_scope. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Signing in needs poppy:browser-sign-in.** The Company MUST reject an access token in step 2 that doesn't have the poppy:browser-sign-in scope, with insufficient_scope as at an API (section 7). | VERIFIED (0.82) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.support-browser-scope` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A Company that lists browser_session_endpoint and offers sign-in supports poppy:browser-sign-in, though it may grant it to fewer clients or Users. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company that lists browser_session_endpoint and offers sign-in MUST support poppy:browser-sign-in (4.7), though it MAY grant it to fewer clients or Users, as for any scope. | VERIFIED (0.93) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.request-browser-scope` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A Personal Agent requests poppy:browser-sign-in only from a Company that lists browser_session_endpoint. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent requests poppy:browser-sign-in only from a Company that lists browser_session_endpoint. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.code-from-page` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent takes the code only from the page its own browser loaded from the endpoint, and does not authorize a code from anywhere else. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST take the code only from the page its own browser loaded from the endpoint in step 1, and MUST NOT authorize a code from anywhere else, such as a message, a conversation, or another page. | VERIFIED (0.91) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.code-outside-model` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent reads the code and sends the authorize request in software its model cannot read. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent reads the code and sends the request in software its model can't read (10.2). | VERIFIED (0.82) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.submit-checks` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — On form submit, the Company checks that the request has the cookie from the page load and that it matches code, and that the code was authorized, has not expired and has not been submitted before; a code is used by the first request that submits it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - The request has the cookie from step 1, and it matches code. - The code was authorized in step 2, hasn't expired, and hasn't been submitted before. | UNVERIFIED (0.47) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.submit-result` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — If every submit check passes, the Company sets its cookie for that Session, clears the join cookie and redirects the browser to a page on its website; otherwise it sets no cookie and returns an error page with an error status. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | If every check passes, the Company sets its cookie for that Session, clears the cookie from step 1, and redirects the browser to a page on its website. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.keep-login` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD NOT · company — In a join without an access token, the Company does not end or change a login the browser already has. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | In a join without an access token, the Company SHOULD NOT end or change that login, because the Personal Agent's browser may have a login it can't easily recreate. | VERIFIED (0.89) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.replace-login` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A join with an access token signs the browser in to the account from the authorize step, with its scopes, in place of any login it had. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A join with an access token signs the browser in to the account from step 2, with its scopes, in place of any login it had, and an earlier login doesn't come back: | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.cookie-only-step-3` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Session's cookie is set only in the form-submit step. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The browser submits the form, and the Company sets the Session's cookie.** The Personal Agent has its browser submit the form: | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.cookie-account` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The cookie acts for the account and scopes authorized in the authorize step, if any, and stops acting for the account when the User signs out or disconnects the Personal Agent. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - MUST be set only in step 3. - MUST act for the account and scopes authorized in step 2, if any, and MUST stop acting for the account when the User signs out or disconnects the Personal Agent (5.5). - MUST NOT outlive the Session. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.apply-scopes` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — After a signed-in join, the website either applies the cookie's scopes, or treats poppy:browser-sign-in as access to everything the account can do on the website. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **What poppy:browser-sign-in allows.** After a signed-in join, the Company's website MUST either apply the cookie's scopes, as the Company's APIs apply an access token's, or treat poppy:browser-sign-in as access to everything the account can do on the website. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.grant-without-ask` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — When the website applies the cookie's scopes, the Company may grant poppy:browser-sign-in without asking the User. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | When the website applies the cookie's scopes, poppy:browser-sign-in allows nothing beyond the token's other scopes, and the Company MAY grant it without asking the User. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.describe-unscoped` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — When poppy:browser-sign-in gives the account's full access on the website, the Company describes it that way, and in Direct and Device Sign-In asks the User to approve it like any other scope. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | When poppy:browser-sign-in gives the account's full access on the website, the Company MUST describe it that way (4.7), such as "Browse the website, and see and do anything your account can do there," and in Direct and Device Sign-In MUST ask the User to approve it like any other scope. | VERIFIED (0.89) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.unscoped-flag` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A Company whose website treats poppy:browser-sign-in as the account's full access sets web.unscoped to true, and lists browser_session_endpoint. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company whose website treats poppy:browser-sign-in as the account's full access MUST set web.unscoped to true in poppy.json, so the Personal Agent knows what the scope allows before requesting it. | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/06-web.md | 6 Web browsing | `web.unscoped-domains` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — If any website of domains that share an issuer is unscoped, each of them sets web.unscoped. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Domains with the same issuer give the scope the same meaning (3.2), so if any of their websites is unscoped, each of them sets web.unscoped. | VERIFIED (0.57) |
| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.accept-session` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Every listed API accepts the Session header and, when the Company offers account access, its access tokens, and enforces their scopes. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Every listed API MUST accept the Session header (5.2) and, when the Company offers account access, its access tokens (4.5), and enforce their scopes. | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.mcp-resource-match` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The resource in the MCP server's protected-resource metadata equals that url. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Domains are compared ignoring case, with no other normalization. \| \| auth \| No \| The Company's sign-in details: issuer, disabled, scopes, and scope_hints, below. \| \| auth.issuer \| If the domain's protected resource metadata lists more than one authorization server \| The Company's OAuth issuer identifier, naming the one Personal Agents use (3.2). | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.mcp-token-only-there` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Each such token works only at the MCP server whose url equals its resource, and the Company rejects it elsewhere. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Each such token works only at the MCP server whose url equals its resource, including when other endpoints share its host. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.mcp-signed-out` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — An MCP server that accepts the Session header may take signed-out calls with the Session header only. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MUST reject it elsewhere. - **Signed out.** An MCP server that accepts the Session header can take signed-out calls with the Session header only, if it allows them. - **Without Session verification.** An MCP server that ignores the Session header can identify the client and account through a validated access token, or serve signed-out requests that use no Session claims. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.mcp-ignore-session` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — An MCP server may ignore the Session header and identify the client and account through a validated access token, or serve signed-out requests that use no Session claims. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MUST reject it elsewhere. - **Signed out.** An MCP server that accepts the Session header can take signed-out calls with the Session header only, if it allows them. - **Without Session verification.** An MCP server that ignores the Session header can identify the client and account through a validated access token, or serve signed-out requests that use no Session claims. | VERIFIED (0.94) |
| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.mcp-authorization-servers` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — When it offers account access, an MCP server's protected-resource metadata includes the Company's issuer in authorization_servers. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company that wants those connections has the server verify the Session header (5.3). - **Other MCP clients.** When it offers account access, the server's protected resource metadata tells MCP clients where to get a token. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.token-errors` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Access-token errors are in WWW-Authenticate, with a challenge for each scheme the resource accepts; an MCP server's challenges include resource_metadata. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Access token errors follow OAuth: they are in the WWW-Authenticate header ([RFC 6750](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750) §3, or [RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) for DPoP-bound tokens), with a challenge for each scheme the resource accepts. | VERIFIED (0.62) |
| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.error-table` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — Other API errors use the section 7 table: a 401 challenge with no error code is handled as insufficient_scope, invalid_token is 401, insufficient_scope is 403 and names the needed scopes, invalid_session and session_ended and version_unsupported are HTTP 400 JSON bodies, and extension_required is HTTP 403 JSON. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Token errors, invalid_session, session_ended, and version_unsupported are the ones in section 7, and rate limits follow 5.6. | UNVERIFIED (0.32) |
| spec/pap-1.0/07-apis.md | 7 APIs | `apis.unverified-identity` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — An unverified Session header does not establish the Personal Agent or the User. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent can have several Sessions for the same User (section 5). \| \| Session header \| The Poppy-Session header on every request a Personal Agent sends to a Company's APIs and endpoints: a JWT the Personal Agent signs for that request, naming the Session and the User ID (5.2). \| \| Delegated key \| A short-lived key a Personal Agent signs its Session JWTs with. | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.1 Conversation protocols | `conversations.description` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company includes a description in every agent.protocols entry. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MUST include a description in every entry, and MAY say the agent can help with anything. | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.1 Conversation protocols | `conversations.description-any` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — The description may say the agent can help with anything. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company MUST include a description in every entry, and MAY say the agent can help with anything. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.1 Conversation protocols | `conversations.skip-unknown` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent skips unfamiliar conversation protocol types. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A delegation, signed by a key in the Personal Agent's jwks_uri, vouches for it (5.2). \| \| Direct Sign-In \| The User signs in on the Company's own page in their browser and approves access (4.2). \| \| Device Sign-In \| The User signs in on the Company's page on any device, using a link and code the Personal Agent shows them (4.3). \| \| **Conversations** \| \| \| Company Agent \| A Company's own agent, r | VERIFIED (0.57) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.2 Requests | `conversations.session` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — Every conversation request carries the Session header, and an access token when signed in. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Every request carries the Session header (5.2), and an access token when signed in (4.5). | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.2 Requests | `conversations.owner` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A request continues a conversation only when the Personal Agent's client_id, the User ID that started it, and, once it uses an account, that account all match; any other request is rejected as the ownership table shows. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A conversation belongs to the Personal Agent's client_id and the User ID that started it, and, once it uses the User's account, to that account too. | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.2 Requests | `conversations.version-mismatch` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — The Company may reject a request on a conversation from a Session that uses a different protocol version or different extension versions, with version_mismatch (HTTP 409) and the protocol version the conversation started in as protocol_version. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Versions.** A request on a conversation can come from a Session that uses a different protocol version, or different extension versions, from the Session that started it (3.4). | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.2 Requests | `conversations.id-alphabet` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — Conversation IDs, request IDs, message IDs and event IDs are strings of 1 to 256 characters from the URL-safe base64 alphabet. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **IDs.** Conversation IDs, request IDs, message IDs, and event IDs are strings of 1 to 256 characters from the URL-safe base64 alphabet ([RFC 4648, section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4648#section-5)): letters, digits, -, and _. | VERIFIED (0.90) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.2 Requests | `conversations.id-format` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · both — The side that did not choose an ID does not depend on its format beyond those rules. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Beyond these rules, the side that chooses an ID decides its format, and the other side MUST NOT depend on it. | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.3 Sending messages | `conversations.create` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A new conversation starts with its first message, except when accepting a user_requested event without a message, and the Company returns the new conversation's ID. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A new conversation starts with its first message, except when accepting a user_requested event without a message (8.10): | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.3 Sending messages | `conversations.request-id` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Every POST body has an id, chosen by the Personal Agent, unique for the User across all its requests; a message's ID is its request's ID. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Request IDs.** Every POST body has an id, chosen by the Personal Agent, which MUST make it unique for the User across all its requests. | VERIFIED (0.92) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.3 Sending messages | `conversations.retry` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — If the Company has already accepted a request with that ID, path and body, it returns the original response and does not act on the request again; if the path or body is different, it returns request_id_conflict. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | If the Company has already accepted a request with that ID, path, and body, it returns the original response and doesn't act on the request again. | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.3 Sending messages | `conversations.order` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company acts on a conversation's messages, handoff requests and handoff cancels in the order it accepts them, whatever their URL; a close is the exception. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Order.** The Company acts on a conversation's messages, handoff requests, and handoff cancels in the order it accepts them, whatever their URL. | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.3 Sending messages | `conversations.accept-only` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A successful POST means the Company accepted the request, not that it has acted on it, and the response has only conversation_id. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Responses.** A successful POST means the Company accepted the request, not that it has acted on it. | VERIFIED (0.78) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.3 Sending messages | `conversations.wait` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — When a POST has wait, the Company accepts the request and then responds as a read of events from the given cursor, or from the start if there is no cursor, holding the request for up to wait seconds, and the status code stays 201 or 202. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company accepts the request, then responds as a read of the conversation's events from that cursor would (8.5), with the conversation's state, holding the request for up to wait seconds. | VERIFIED (0.63) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.ignore-client-fields` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Personal Agent does not send id or role, and the Company ignores them if sent. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent doesn't send id or role, and the Company ignores them if sent. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.message-content` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — A message has at least one of text, data, context or extensions. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A message MUST have at least one of text, data, context, or extensions. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.as-of-request` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company Agent message with author.type agent may include as_of_request, the ID of the latest Personal Agent request it took into account. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - as_of_request: a message from the Company Agent, with author.type agent, MAY include the ID of the latest message, handoff request, or handoff cancel from the Personal Agent that it took into account. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.as-of-cursor` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · agent — A Personal Agent message with author.type agent may include as_of_cursor, the cursor of the last read it had handled. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Taking a request into account doesn't mean the Company acted on it, because a close can drop requests (8.12). - as_of_cursor: a message the Personal Agent wrote, with author.type agent, MAY include the cursor of the last read it had handled when it wrote the message (8.5), if it has one. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.as-of-human` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · both — A message with author.type human does not include as_of_request or as_of_cursor. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company sets it when it records the message as an event (8.5). \| \| as_of_request \| No \| On a message from the Company Agent, the ID of the latest request it took into account (below). \| \| as_of_cursor \| No \| On a message the Personal Agent wrote, the last cursor it had read (below). \| \| author \| Yes \| Who wrote the message. type is agent if the Personal Agent wrote it, human if the | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.as-of-optional` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · both — The Company and the Personal Agent do not depend on either field being present, and treat each only as a hint. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company and the Personal Agent MUST NOT depend on either field being present, and treat each only as a hint for interpreting the message. | VERIFIED (0.91) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.context-format` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — When a Personal Agent shares locale, time_zone or user_available, it uses the field and format defined in section 8.4. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | \| Personal Agent Protocol conversation ([spec 8](/docs/1.0/spec#conversations)) \| UCP Ask \| \| --- \| --- \| \| Conversation, with a Company-chosen conversation_id \| conversation.id, Company-returned; replay it on follow-ups \| \| Conversation lifetime \| conversation.expires_at, Company-chosen \| \| Message text \| query \| \| Message data \| ids: the resources the question is about \| \| Message  | UNVERIFIED (0.54) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.context-extra` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · agent — context may include other fields, and a Company may ignore them. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | From 1.0, a newer minor version won't break older Personal Agents, which use the document and ignore fields they don't recognize. \| \| supported_versions \| No \| The other protocol versions the Company serves, each mapped to the URL of that version's document (3.4). \| \| organization \| Yes \| The Company's display name, its domain, and an optional logo_uri, an HTTPS URL of the Company's logo | VERIFIED (0.57) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.context-null` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — A context field set to null clears it, and fields a message leaves out keep their previous values. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Any message can carry context, and fields it leaves out keep their previous values. | VERIFIED (0.64) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.author-type` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — author.type is agent or human. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company sets it when it records the message as an event (8.5). \| \| as_of_request \| No \| On a message from the Company Agent, the ID of the latest request it took into account (below). \| \| as_of_cursor \| No \| On a message the Personal Agent wrote, the last cursor it had read (below). \| \| author \| Yes \| Who wrote the message. type is agent if the Personal Agent wrote it, human if the | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.ignore-author-display` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A Company ignores author.id, name and image on the Personal Agent's messages. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - type is required: agent or human, as 8.8 describes. - id is a stable ID for the author, chosen by the Company, so the Personal Agent can tell the same author's messages apart from others'. - name is the author's display name, such as Alex. - image is the URL of the author's picture, for the Personal Agent to show next to the name. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.author-display` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company whose messages come from several people or AI personas may set author.id, name and image. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company whose messages come from several people or AI personas MAY use them to say which one wrote each message. | VERIFIED (0.70) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.unknown-role` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A Personal Agent treats a message with a role it does not recognize as content from the Company. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent treats a message with a role it doesn't recognize as content from the Company (10.1). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.unknown-author-type` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · both — Either side treats an author.type it does not recognize as agent. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Either side treats an author.type it doesn't recognize as agent, so a message is never taken to be a person's own words unless it says so. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.4 Message fields | `conversations.extension-version` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · both — A side does not send an extension's content unless the other side uses that extension with the same version. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A side MUST NOT send an extension's content unless the other side uses that extension with the same version: the Company checks the request's Poppy-Extensions (3.4), and the Personal Agent checks the Company's document for the version it chose (3.1). | VERIFIED (0.90) |
| spec/pap-1.0/08-conversations.md | 8.5 Reading events | `conversations.skip-events` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Personal Agents skip event types they do not recognize. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Personal Agents MUST skip event types they don't recognize, so later versions and extensions can add types. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
