| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Sending the approval | `ops.keep-recorded` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company keeps a recorded approval that is not used up until the approved revision's expires_at, and does not apply it to requests from a Session that cannot approve the operation. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | The Company MUST keep a recorded approval that isn't used up until the approved revision's expires_at, and MUST NOT apply it to requests from a Session that can't approve the operation. | VERIFIED (0.87) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Sending the approval | `ops.checks` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — Before performing the action, the Company checks that the Session can approve the operation, that it has not already been performed, and that the revision is one it returned, has not expired, and the request asks for exactly what it describes, and responds as the first failing check says. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | The revision is one the Company returned for this operation, it hasn't expired, and the request asks for exactly what it describes. | UNVERIFIED (0.43) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Sending the approval | `ops.inconsequential` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — The Company may ignore differences it considers inconsequential, and may treat a revision it has replaced as failing the revision check. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | The Company compares what the requests mean, not their bytes, and MAY ignore differences it considers inconsequential, such as the order of JSON members. | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Sending the approval | `ops.no-same-revision` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — A Company does not answer an approval with the revision it names. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | A Company MUST NOT answer an approval with the revision it names, so a Personal Agent never retries the same approval in a loop. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Sending the approval | `ops.decline` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — Once the checks pass, a Company may decline the approval instead of performing the action, performs nothing, and returns no new operation. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | Once the checks pass, a Company MAY decline the approval instead of performing the action, for its own reasons, such as approver, the account, or its own risk checks. | VERIFIED (0.69) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Sending the approval | `ops.decline-reason` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — The Company does not put anything in reason that the Session could not already see. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | The Company MUST NOT put anything in reason that the Session couldn't already see. | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Sending the approval | `ops.no-record-declined` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — The Company does not record a declined approval. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | The Company MUST NOT record a declined approval, so a website never finds it for a later request. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Sending the approval | `ops.no-resend-declined` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — When an approval is declined, the Personal Agent does not send that approval again, and does not approve a new revision for the same action without asking the User. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | When an approval is declined, the Personal Agent MUST NOT send that approval again, and MUST NOT approve a new revision for the same action without asking the User (5). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.7 Retries and at most once | `ops.remember-performed` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company remembers that it performed an operation at least until the approved revision's expires_at, and while it is being performed. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | - The Company MUST perform each operation at most once, however many times its approval is sent. - A request with the approval of an operation already performed gets the response the Company gave the first time, or one that says the same, for as long as the Company remembers the operation. - The Company MUST remember that it performed an operation at least until the approved revision's expires_at | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.7 Retries and at most once | `ops.forget-after` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — After that, the Company may forget it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | If the Personal Agent can't tell whether it does, it asks the User. - If the Company answers the Personal Agent's agent approval with a new revision (6), or a website returns a new operation for the action after recording it, the Personal Agent MUST NOT approve the new one on the same instruction. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.7 Retries and at most once | `ops.retry-same-response` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A request with the approval of an operation already performed gets the response the Company gave the first time, or one that says the same, for as long as the Company remembers the operation. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | - The Company MUST perform each operation at most once, however many times its approval is sent. - A request with the approval of an operation already performed gets the response the Company gave the first time, or one that says the same, for as long as the Company remembers the operation. - The Company MUST remember that it performed an operation at least until the approved revision's expires_at | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.7 Retries and at most once | `ops.unknown-not-failed` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — While an operation is being performed, or the Company does not know whether it took effect, the Company does not respond as if it failed. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | In a conversation, a message retried with the same ID gets the original response ([spec 8.3](/docs/1.0/spec#sending-messages)), and a new message with the same approval adds nothing. - While an operation is being performed, or the Company doesn't know whether it took effect, for example after a timeout in one of its own systems, the Company MUST NOT respond as if it failed. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.7 Retries and at most once | `ops.no-new-until-known` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — If a request with an approval fails or times out, the Personal Agent sends it again with the same approval, and does not approve a new operation for the same action, or ask the User to, until it knows whether the first one was performed. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | It MUST NOT approve a new operation for the same action, or ask the User to, until it knows whether the first one was performed. | VERIFIED (0.56) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-operations.md | ops.8 Security considerations | `ops.website-check-action` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — On a website, the Personal Agent checks that the operation is for the action its browser just took, and points out any difference to the User. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | The terms come from the Company, so the User sees the real terms, but the Personal Agent SHOULD check that the operation is for the action its browser just took, and point out any difference to the User, as with terms (5). | VERIFIED (0.75) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 2 UCP advertising support | `ucp.same-issuer` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — The Company's UCP services support account access through the issuer this protocol selected. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | For this extension, the Personal Agent selects the issuer as spec 3.2 describes, including auth.issuer when that document lists more than one authorization server, and the Company's UCP services SHOULD support account access through that issuer. | VERIFIED (0.78) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 2 UCP advertising support | `ucp.other-issuer` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — If a UCP service requires a different issuer, the Personal Agent does not use that issuer through this extension. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | If a UCP service requires a different issuer, the Personal Agent MUST NOT use that issuer through this extension. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 2 UCP advertising support | `ucp.session-header` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent sends the Session header to the Company's UCP services, which this extension defines as protocol endpoints. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | Its UCP identity linking stays available to UCP used independently of this extension (below). - Support means that the Company's UCP services, at the endpoints its profile lists, are endpoints this extension defines: the Personal Agent sends them the Session header ([spec 5.2](/docs/1.0/spec#session-header)), and the Company handles its requests as UCP defines (4, 8). | VERIFIED (0.91) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 2 UCP advertising support | `ucp.declare` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent that supports this extension declares "ucp";v="1" in Poppy-Extensions. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A Personal Agent that supports this extension SHOULD declare "ucp";v="1" in Poppy-Extensions ([spec 3.4](/docs/1.0/spec#versions)), so a Company can tell from its other requests that the Personal Agent speaks UCP, for example to point it to UCP from another of its interfaces. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 2 UCP advertising support | `ucp.list-mcp` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may also list its UCP MCP endpoint in apis. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A Company MAY also list its UCP MCP endpoint in apis, for MCP clients that speak UCP but don't know this extension. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 2 UCP advertising support | `ucp.no-require-poppy` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · company — A Company does not require a UCP platform to implement this protocol as a condition of access UCP otherwise permits. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | **UCP without this extension.** A Company MUST NOT require a UCP platform to implement this protocol as a condition of access UCP otherwise permits. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 2 UCP advertising support | `ucp.independent` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · agent — Software that implements this protocol may also use UCP with the Company independently of this extension. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | Software that implements this protocol MAY also use UCP with the Company independently of this extension, with any mechanism UCP permits, such as the Accelerated IdP Flow. | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 3 UCP discovery and negotiation | `ucp.no-redirect` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — The Personal Agent does not follow a redirect from https://{organization.domain}/.well-known/ucp, and does not use a UCP profile from another host for this Company. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | The Personal Agent MUST NOT follow a redirect from this URL, and MUST NOT use a UCP profile from another host for this Company, as [UCP requires](https://ucp.dev/latest/specification/overview/#hosting). | VERIFIED (0.69) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 3 UCP discovery and negotiation | `ucp.profile-not-per-user` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — A platform profile is not specific to a User and does not say anything about a User or the Companies they use. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | Each turn is authorized on its own, as UCP requires and as [spec 8.11](/docs/1.0/spec#conversation-sign-in) requires for this protocol's conversations. - **Profiles aren't per User.** A platform profile specific to a User would let Companies match that User across Companies by its URL, which User IDs prevent ([spec 5.1](/docs/1.0/spec#user-ids)) (3). - **Payments.** UCP handles payment through pay | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.same-profile-url` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Where a transport carries the platform profile in more than one place, the Personal Agent sends the same URL in each. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | Where a transport carries the profile in more than one place, the Personal Agent MUST send the same URL in each, and the Company MUST reject a request where they differ; otherwise a request could authenticate as one platform and negotiate as another. | VERIFIED (0.91) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.reject-profile-mismatch` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company rejects a request where those profile URLs differ. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | Where a transport carries the profile in more than one place, the Personal Agent MUST send the same URL in each, and the Company MUST reject a request where they differ; otherwise a request could authenticate as one platform and negotiate as another. | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.operator-signature` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — The Personal Agent signs UCP requests with an HTTP Message Signature, as it should for every request that carries the Session header. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | This extends UCP's [identity binding](https://ucp.dev/latest/specification/overview/#identity-binding), which rejects a request whose authenticated identity conflicts with UCP-Agent. - Poppy-Session, the Session header, as on any endpoint this protocol or an extension defines ([spec 5.2](/docs/1.0/spec#session-header)). - An operator signature: the Personal Agent SHOULD sign UCP requests with  | VERIFIED (0.92) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.verify-session` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may verify the Session header on UCP requests. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A Company MAY verify the Session header on UCP requests and use it, for example to recognize a returning signed-out User; it MUST verify the header before relying on any Session claim, and a UCP service that uses no Session claims can ignore it (8). | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.verify-before-claims` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company verifies the header before relying on any Session claim. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A Company MAY verify the Session header on UCP requests and use it, for example to recognize a returning signed-out User; it MUST verify the header before relying on any Session claim, and a UCP service that uses no Session claims can ignore it (8). | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.bearer` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — When a UCP request is signed in, the access token is a Bearer token, and the Company returns token_type Bearer for tokens for its UCP services. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | The Company returns token_type Bearer for tokens for its UCP services, as [spec 4.5](/docs/1.0/spec#access-tokens) allows for a service that doesn't accept DPoP, for example for a resource that names them, and the Personal Agent sends each token as its token_type names. | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.one-sign-in` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A Company that offers both this protocol's sign-in and UCP identity linking offers Direct Sign-In, and lists private_key_jwt in token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | - A Company that offers both this protocol's sign-in and identity linking makes them one: it MUST offer Direct Sign-In, and MUST list private_key_jwt in its issuer's token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported, so a Personal Agent can authenticate as [spec 4.1](/docs/1.0/spec#agent-identity) describes. - The Company MUST list each scope in its identity linking config.scopes in scopes_supported,  | VERIFIED (0.93) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.scopes-listed` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — The Company lists each scope in its identity-linking config.scopes in scopes_supported. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | - A Company that offers both this protocol's sign-in and identity linking makes them one: it MUST offer Direct Sign-In, and MUST list private_key_jwt in its issuer's token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported, so a Personal Agent can authenticate as [spec 4.1](/docs/1.0/spec#agent-identity) describes. - The Company MUST list each scope in its identity linking config.scopes in scopes_supported,  | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.describe-scopes` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — The Company describes each such scope and gives auth.scope_hints for it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Recommended for scopes no OpenAPI description the Company lists describes, and used over an OpenAPI description of the same scope (4.7). \| \| auth.scope_hints \| No \| Hints about what each scope allows, keyed by scope name: changes and personal_data (4.7). | VERIFIED (0.83) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.no-foreign-token` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — A Personal Agent does not send a token it got independently of this extension on a request that carries the Session header. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A Personal Agent MUST NOT send a token it got independently of this extension on a request that carries the Session header. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 4 UCP identity and authorization | `ucp.insufficient-scope` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — For UCP insufficient_scope, the Personal Agent requests every scope it still needs, including scopes it already has and wants to keep, and the Company grants only the scopes the request names. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | **Requesting more scopes.** For insufficient_scope, the Personal Agent requests every scope it still needs, as [spec 4.7](/docs/1.0/spec#scopes) describes: the scopes the challenge names, and the scopes it already has and wants to keep, including scopes for the Company's other APIs. | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 5 UCP conversations through Ask | `ucp.ask-listed` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A Company that lists a ucp conversation entry lists this extension, and its UCP profile offers dev.ucp.common.ask. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | The Personal Agent finds dev.ucp.common.ask among the profile's capabilities, and reaches it at the dev.ucp.common service's endpoint for a transport it supports. - A Company that lists a ucp entry MUST list this extension, and its UCP profile MUST offer dev.ucp.common.ask. - Requests follow UCP's Ask bindings and section 4 of this page, not [spec 8.2–8.13](/docs/1.0/spec#conversation-requ | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 5 UCP conversations through Ask | `ucp.ask-conversation` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may return a conversation so follow-up questions build on earlier turns. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A Company MAY return a conversation so follow-up questions build on earlier turns. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 5 UCP conversations through Ask | `ucp.also-poppy` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may list a poppy conversation entry too. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | The Personal Agent finds dev.ucp.common.ask among the profile's capabilities, and reaches it at the dev.ucp.common service's endpoint for a transport it supports. - A Company that lists a ucp entry MUST list this extension, and its UCP profile MUST offer dev.ucp.common.ask. - Requests follow UCP's Ask bindings and section 4 of this page, not [spec 8.2–8.13](/docs/1.0/spec#conversation-requ | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 6 UCP commerce and approval | `ucp.no-double-approval` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD NOT · company — A Company does not also return an operation for a UCP capability's operation, unless a negotiated UCP extension defines how an operation rides in a UCP response. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | That is the one approval for a purchase, so a Company SHOULD NOT also return an [operation](/docs/1.0/extensions/operations) for a UCP capability's operation, unless a UCP extension the two have negotiated defines how an operation rides in a UCP response; two approval records for one action can disagree. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 6 UCP commerce and approval | `ucp.stray-operation` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — A Personal Agent that receives an operation outside such an extension treats it as content and approves nothing. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A Personal Agent that receives one outside such an extension treats it as content ([spec 10.1](/docs/1.0/spec#untrusted-content)) and approves nothing. - **Retries.** UCP's idempotency keys and checkout lifecycle give a retried completion the first result. | VERIFIED (0.90) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 7 UCP escalation | `ucp.continue-url` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent presents continue_url in the User's browser, or in UCP's embedded checkout where both sides support it, and sends no token or Session header to it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | \| Request \| What the Personal Agent sends \| What it gets \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| Signed out \| The Session header; no access token \| What the Company offers without an account, as its UCP profile and access policy allow: catalog search and lookup, policies, Ask, carts, and guest checkout \| \| Signed in \| The Session header and an access token ([spec 4.5](/docs/1.0/spec#access-tokens)) \| The same, pe | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 7 UCP escalation | `ucp.browser-endpoint` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A Company may offer web.browser_session_endpoint; a continue_url does not use it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A Company MAY offer web.browser_session_endpoint for its site as at any Company; a continue_url doesn't use it. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 8 UCP sessions and resources | `ucp.continue-later` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · agent — The Personal Agent may continue a UCP workflow in a later Session for the same User while the workflow remains available and the Company's access policy allows. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A workflow can outlive the Session that created it: the Personal Agent MAY continue it in a later Session for the same User while the workflow remains available and the Company's access policy allows. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 8 UCP sessions and resources | `ucp.no-reuse-id` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — The Personal Agent does not reuse one User's UCP workflow identifier for another User. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | It MUST NOT reuse one User's UCP workflow identifier for another User. | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 8 UCP sessions and resources | `ucp.no-assume-visible` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST NOT · agent — A Personal Agent does not assume that a cart or checkout it created through UCP appears on the Company's website, in a Company Agent conversation, or in another Session unless the Company says so. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | Grouping changes nothing about the workflows themselves, and this extension doesn't expect it: a Personal Agent MUST NOT assume that a cart or checkout it created through UCP appears on the Company's website, in a Company Agent conversation, or in another Session unless the Company says so. - A UCP endpoint MAY instead ignore the Session header, as [spec 7](/docs/1.0/spec#apis) allows an MCP serve | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 8 UCP sessions and resources | `ucp.ignore-or-require` | (not in pap-0.3) | MAY · company — A UCP endpoint may ignore the Session header, and a Company may also require a verified Session header on requests through this extension. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A Company MAY also require a verified Session header on requests through this extension, and rely on its claims (4). | VERIFIED (0.80) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 9 UCP errors | `ucp.session-errors` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · company — A UCP endpoint that verifies the Session header returns invalid_session and session_ended as UCP responses: HTTP 400 with UCP's error body and code set to the error's name over REST, and a JSON-RPC error whose data.code names it over MCP. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | A UCP endpoint returns them as UCP responses, not in the specification's JSON body: HTTP 400 with UCP's error body and code set to the error's name over REST, and a JSON-RPC error whose data.code names it over MCP. | VERIFIED (0.70) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 9 UCP errors | `ucp.handle-session-errors` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — For session_ended the Personal Agent starts a new Session and sends the request again, once, and for invalid_session it stops sending the request until it fixes the cause. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | The Personal Agent handles them as section 7 of the specification says: for session_ended it starts a new Session and sends the request again, once, and for invalid_session it stops sending the request until it fixes the cause. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 10 UCP security | `ucp.one-issuer` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Through this extension, the Personal Agent signs in only at the issuer it selected, whatever realm or resource_metadata a UCP challenge carries. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | - **One issuer, one document.** Through this extension, the Personal Agent uses the issuer it selected from the protected resource metadata at organization.domain (2). | VERIFIED (0.62) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 10 UCP security | `ucp.content` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Product data, messages and Ask answers are content from the Company: data, never instructions, and never the User's approval of a purchase. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | UCP's authority binding shows who published each schema the profile names, not that the Company should be trusted. - **Content from the Company.** Product data, messages, and Ask answers are content from the Company ([spec 10.1](/docs/1.0/spec#untrusted-content)): data, never instructions, and never the User's approval of a purchase. | VERIFIED (0.92) |
| spec/pap-1.0/ext-ucp.md | 10 UCP security | `ucp.payment-secrets` | (not in pap-0.3) | MUST · agent — Payment-handler tokens and any credential the Personal Agent handles are secrets kept out of model context. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | Those tokens, and any credential the Personal Agent does handle, are secrets, kept out of model context with the others in [spec 10.2](/docs/1.0/spec#secrets). - **Webhooks.** A UCP platform profile can name a webhook URL for order updates. | VERIFIED (0.73) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.assertion-exp` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — Set a client assertion's exp at most five minutes after its iat. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Allow a small clock skew, such as 30 seconds, and reject assertions that claim a long lifetime. - Make the jti of each client assertion, delegation, Session JWT, and DPoP proof at least 128 random bits, and each Session ID too. - Personal agents: set a client assertion's exp at most five minutes after its iat. - Companies: reject a reused client assertion jti from the same client_id unti | VERIFIED (0.86) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.replay-jti` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Reject a reused client-assertion jti from the same client_id until it expires. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Allow a small clock skew, such as 30 seconds, and reject assertions that claim a long lifetime. - Make the jti of each client assertion, delegation, Session JWT, and DPoP proof at least 128 random bits, and each Session ID too. - Personal agents: set a client assertion's exp at most five minutes after its iat. - Companies: reject a reused client assertion jti from the same client_id unti | VERIFIED (0.88) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.operator-signature` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · both — Build the body, any DPoP proof and the Session JWT before the operator signature; keep operator keys separate from delegated Session keys; do not send devices a shared operator private key; verify the Session JWT's exact iss before accepting the client; test the edge and origin together and protect the origin from forged forwarding headers. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Verify the Session JWT's exact iss before accepting it as that client, then apply Company policy and account authorization. - Test the actual edge and origin together: registration, directory caching, key rotation, accepted signature components, body handling, and forwarded authentication results. | UNVERIFIED (0.41) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.explicit-replies` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · both — Make an answer or approval explicit about the question or action it addresses, and treat a short reply that could answer more than one open question as answering none of them. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Both parties SHOULD make an answer or approval explicit about the question or action it addresses. - Both parties SHOULD treat a short reply that could answer more than one open question as answering none of them, and ask which one is meant before acting. | VERIFIED (1.00) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.both For both parties | `guide.mcp-ucp` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · both — Follow the MCP lifecycle of the negotiated version, keep Poppy sid distinct from Mcp-Session-Id, cache MCP results per User and Session when those affect the result, and do not assume one token or signature works for both Poppy and UCP. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Keep cached results specific to the Poppy User and Session when those affect the result; account scopes alone are not an adequate cache key. | UNVERIFIED (0.44) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.versions` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — Change protocol and extension versions only at a new Session, declare only extensions the Company lists, and record the versions each conversation started in so a version_mismatch can be finished in a Session that still uses them. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **Versions.** A request on a conversation can come from a Session that uses a different protocol version, or different extension versions, from the Session that started it (3.4). | UNVERIFIED (0.44) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.session-header` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — Decide Session-header destinations from poppy.json and listed API descriptions, keep the header off discovery and OAuth requests, sign a new JWT per request where the final method and URL are known, round delegation iat down, and on invalid_session fix the JWT instead of starting a new Session. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | UNVERIFIED (0.40) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.plan-access` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — Read OpenAPI security requirements and MCP authorization discovery before calling, use a refresh token that covers the task before asking the User to sign in, and do not automatically reconnect an account the User disconnected. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Use a refresh token that covers the task before asking the User to sign in, and do not automatically reconnect an account the User disconnected. | VERIFIED (0.60) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.agent-operations` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · agent — Show the Company's summary or every value in terms, check terms against what was asked, approve only on the User's instruction, send the saved request with the approval, and on a website take Poppy-Operation only from the browser's responses. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/browser-control | \| \| Operations \| Browser control \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| Who takes the step \| The Personal Agent, once the terms are approved \| The User, in the Personal Agent's browser \| \| What the User does \| Approves the terms in the Personal Agent's own interface, or gives the Personal Agent an instruction that covers them \| Takes over the browser and uses the Company's page \| \| Interfaces \| APIs, MCP servers | UNVERIFIED (0.46) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.adoption` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Publish poppy.json with at least one channel, then add signed-out APIs, Session verification, OAuth, browser join, excluded tasks, feedback and extensions independently as the tasks need them. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It is signed with a delegated key, which a key in the Personal Agent's jwks_uri vouches for in a delegation carried in the JWT's poppy_dlg header (5.2). - **Interactions above OAuth:** browser join, with its poppy:browser-sign-in scope (section 6), APIs (section 7), conversations (section 8), excluded tasks, skills, and feedback (section 9), and extensions (3.3), including [Operations](/docs | UNVERIFIED (0.53) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.serve-versions` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Publish each protocol version as its own document on the same host, tell endpoints apart by Poppy-Version, and retire a version with Deprecation and Sunset before removing it. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | A Deprecation or Sunset header on a response is a sign to move that company to a newer version before the date it gives. | UNVERIFIED (0.31) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.signed-out-no-oauth` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Offer signed-out Sessions without an authorization server by returning 404 or empty authorization_servers, and set auth.disabled rather than dropping an issuer you are not ready to open. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | VERIFIED (0.59) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.company-agents` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Fetch client_id without following redirects, cache client metadata, keep allowlists or blocklists, and publish client_id_metadata_document_supported when the authorization server accepts client ID metadata documents. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Your resources also fetch it when verifying Session JWTs ([5.3](/docs/1.0/spec#session-verification)). - If your authorization server accepts client ID metadata documents, publish client_id_metadata_document_supported: true in its metadata, as the [CIMD draft](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-oauth-client-id-metadata-document/) §8 defines, so other clients, such as MCP clients, can te | VERIFIED (0.56) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.verify-session` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Verify the Session header in one place, cache a verified delegation by a hash of the whole delegation, compare htu with the configured public origin, use about a one-minute iat window, and do not use unverified claims for attribution or state. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Build it from your configured public origin, not from the Host header your server receives. - Use an acceptance window of about one minute for iat, plus a small clock skew. - Rejecting a reused jti is optional, as it is for DPoP proofs, because servers behind one endpoint don't always share state. | UNVERIFIED (0.35) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.sessions` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Share Session state across servers, record the User ID and Agent ID a Session starts with, remember ended sids, and use separate Sessions for separate accounts. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Behind a load balancer, share session state across the servers that handle it, as with server-side cookie sessions, so requests reach the same state whichever server handles them. - Record the user ID and agent ID a session starts with, and reject later Session JWTs for it with different ones ([5.3](/docs/1.0/spec#session-verification)). - Session verification needs no state until the session lo | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.lifetimes` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Give access tokens a lifetime of hours, invalidate them at sign-out or keep them to a few minutes, do not stop accepted work because an access token expired, and bind refresh tokens to their DPoP key where account access has meaningful consequences. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | If you can do neither, keep access tokens to a few minutes ([5.5](/docs/1.0/spec#ending-sessions)). - Don't stop accepted work because the access token that started it expired. | UNVERIFIED (0.43) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.api-descriptions` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — If you offer Direct or Device Sign-In, make scopes and APIs cover the tasks Users ask for, and write API and MCP descriptions for the model that reads them. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | If you offer direct or device sign-in, make your scopes and APIs cover the tasks users ask personal agents to do. | VERIFIED (0.67) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.company-browsing` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Choose one of the four website levels, and if you offer OAuth and allow browsing, list browser_session_endpoint; make join codes at least 128 bits, use the __Host-poppy_join cookie, and send the page with no-store, frame-ancestors 'none' and Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Send the page with Cache-Control: no-store, Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none', and Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin, so caches don't keep it, other pages can't frame it, and the page that opened it can't keep a reference to its window ([section 6](/docs/1.0/spec#web-browsing)). | UNVERIFIED (0.38) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.internal` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Verify access tokens and Session JWTs at the edge, and do not forward them from one internal service to another. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Don't forward an access token or Session JWT from one of your services to another. - Verify them at the edge, then pass the verified client_id, session, user ID, account, and scopes to internal services through a gateway or service credentials. - Internal credentials used for a request shouldn't allow more than the access token does. | VERIFIED (0.73) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.conversation-provider` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Point agent.protocols at the provider's endpoint, publish that URL as its own protected resource, and keep the Session JWT audience as the Company's identifier. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - List the conversation endpoint in agent.protocols in the company's poppy.json, with a description of what the provider's agent can help with for that company. - Use that endpoint URL as the service's resource identifier, including the tenant path so tokens for different companies have different audiences ([RFC 8707 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-3)). | UNVERIFIED (0.54) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.less-access` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Limit scopes by client, refuse a sign-in type with unauthorized_client, or set auth.disabled, and choose the issuer before launch. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | These lists are discovery, not enforcement: agents can request scopes documented by an API or sign-in extension too. - Refuse sign-in, or one sign-in type, to a personal agent with unauthorized_client from the grant's endpoint, and keep accepting it for signed-out sessions ([4.1](/docs/1.0/spec#agent-identity)). grant_types_supported lists the grants every client can see, so a refusal by clie | VERIFIED (0.71) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.guest` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Let a signed-out Session do what a signed-out visitor can do, and limit abuse with rate limits by client_id. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Let a signed-out personal agent session do what a signed-out visitor can do on your website, such as search products, read store policies, or ask the company agent general questions. | UNVERIFIED (0.50) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.company-operations` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Put everything that affects the action in terms, send a summary when you want your wording shown, derive downstream idempotency keys from the operation ID, and pass a 403 with Poppy-Operation through unchanged. | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Personal agents show terms to users, or describe them in their own words ([Operations 3](/docs/1.0/extensions/operations#operation-object)). - Send a summary when you want users to read your wording. | UNVERIFIED (0.35) |
| spec/pap-1.0/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.errors` | (not in pap-0.3) | SHOULD · company — Keep error_description free of account details, and return the same error for "does not exist" and "belongs to someone else". | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Keep error_description and other error text free of account details and other users' data. - Return the same error for "doesn't exist" and "belongs to someone else", as conversation_not_found does ([8.13](/docs/1.0/spec#conversation-errors)). | VERIFIED (0.92) |

## Removed 0.3 ids

These ids are not in `spec/pap-1.0`. Absence is verified by that file set. A removal is not by itself a quote that the protocol deleted the duty. Where the closest draft 1.0 sentence scores under 0.35, the row says the duty was not found in the 2026-11-14 snapshot and is UNVERIFIED as a deliberate deletion (it may have moved into a sentence this matcher missed). Where the score is at least 0.35, the quote is the closest surviving sentence and the status is UNVERIFIED as a mapping.

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sessions.md | 4.2 Starting a Session | `sessions.token-hours` | SHOULD · company — Session Tokens last hours, not days. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Give access tokens a lifetime of hours, not days. - At sign-out, invalidate the access tokens issued under the revoked refresh token, as [RFC 7009](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7009) §2.1 describes, or check on each request whether the authorization is still active, so sign-out takes effect right away. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.60) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sessions.md | 4.2 Starting a Session | `sessions.renew-same-owner` | MUST · company — On renewal the Company finds the Session by the proof's key, checks that it belongs to the same client_id and User ID as the assertion, and returns invalid_grant for a key that names a Session of a different client_id or User ID. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.44) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sessions.md | 4.2 Starting a Session | `sessions.errors` | MUST · company — Token endpoint errors use the OAuth error response: invalid_client 401, invalid_grant 400, account_mismatch 400, invalid_target 400, invalid_dpop_proof and use_dpop_nonce 400, extension_required 403, rate_limited 429 with Retry-After. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.33) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sessions.md | 4.3 Using Session Tokens | `dpop.proof-claims` | MUST · both — A proof has jti, htm and htu (the request's method and URL without the query or fragment), iat, ath (the SHA-256 hash of the Session Token, absent at the token endpoint and in a browser-session form), and nonce when required. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It uses the same characters as Conversation IDs (8.2). - sub is the User ID for this Company (5.1). - agent_id is the optional Agent ID (5.1). - htm and htu are the request's method and URL, without the query or fragment, as in a DPoP proof ([RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) §4.2). - iat is when the Personal Agent signed it, in Unix seconds, and jti is unique and ungue | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.53) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sessions.md | 4.3 Using Session Tokens | `dpop.verify` | MUST · company — The Company validates every proof as RFC 9449 §4.3 describes: exactly one DPoP header holding a well-formed JWT, typ dpop+jwt, an asymmetric alg it accepts and never none, a jwk with no private key, a signature that verifies with it, matching htm and htu, made within its acceptance window, a matching nonce when it requires one, and on requests with a Session Token the bound key and a matching ath. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.33) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sessions.md | 4.3 Using Session Tokens | `dpop.replay-token-endpoint` | MUST · company — The token endpoint rejects a proof whose jti it has already accepted within the acceptance window. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It then rejects a refresh whose proof is missing or from another key with invalid_grant, and issues nothing bound to that key, except through such an extension's replacement procedure. [RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) §5 leaves a confidential client's refresh tokens unbound, because client authentication already limits who can use them. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.40) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.prefer-delegated` | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent uses Direct or Device Sign-In rather than Mediated Sign-In when the Company offers it and its scopes cover the task. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in | A Personal Agent SHOULD use Direct or Device Sign-In rather than Mediated Sign-In when the Company offers it and its scopes cover the task. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.92) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.scope-namespace` | MUST NOT · company — Company scope names do not start with poppy:, which is reserved for this protocol. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | \| Term \| Meaning \| \| --- \| --- \| \| **Parties** \| \| \| User \| The person a Personal Agent acts for. \| \| Personal Agent \| Software acting for the User. \| \| Company \| An entity that implements the protocol so Personal Agents can work with it. \| \| **Discovery** \| \| \| poppy.json \| The JSON document a Company publishes at /.well-known/poppy.json to tell Personal Agents how to start Sessions and sign  | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.57) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.scopes-from-metadata` | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent requests only scopes from scopes_supported. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The protected resource metadata can also list the scopes a Personal Agent can request in scopes_supported ([RFC 9728](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728) §2), instead of or as well as the issuer's metadata (below, 4.7). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.80) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.describe-scopes` | MUST · company — The Company describes each scope in scopes_supported, in the scopes of an OAuth 2.0 security scheme in an OpenAPI description it lists, or otherwise in auth.scopes. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - The Company SHOULD describe each scope it offers Personal Agents, so the Personal Agent can choose the ones a task needs: in the scopes of an OAuth 2.0 security scheme in an OpenAPI description it lists (section 7), or otherwise in auth.scopes. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.85) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.scope-hints` | MAY · company — A Company may give hints about any scope in scopes_supported in auth.scope_hints, keyed by scope name, for the most consequential operation that requires it: changes is none, reversible or irreversible, and personal_data is a boolean. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Recommended for scopes no OpenAPI description the Company lists describes, and used over an OpenAPI description of the same scope (4.7). \| \| auth.scope_hints \| No \| Hints about what each scope allows, keyed by scope name: changes and personal_data (4.7). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.scope-hints-fields` | MUST · company — Each auth.scope_hints entry includes both changes and personal_data. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Recommended for scopes no OpenAPI description the Company lists describes, and used over an OpenAPI description of the same scope (4.7). \| \| auth.scope_hints \| No \| Hints about what each scope allows, keyed by scope name: changes and personal_data (4.7). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.67) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.scope-hints-unknown` | MUST · agent — A Personal Agent ignores hint fields it doesn't recognize, treats a changes value it doesn't recognize as irreversible, and treats a scope with no entry as having no hints. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent MUST ignore fields it doesn't recognize, and treat a changes value it doesn't recognize as irreversible, so later versions can add both. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.scope-enforcement` | MUST · company — Companies decide which operations require each scope and enforce them across web browsing, APIs and actions performed by the Company Agent. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - Companies decide which actions require each scope and enforce them across APIs and actions performed by the Company Agent. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.85) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.scope-param` | MUST · agent — Each sign-in request includes scope as a space-separated string following OAuth scope syntax. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - Each sign-in request MUST include scope, except where an extension continues an earlier request, as a space-separated string, following [OAuth scope syntax](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.3). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.90) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.least-scopes` | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent requests only the scopes the User's tasks need. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - A Personal Agent SHOULD request only the scopes the User's tasks need, following [RFC 9700](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9700#section-2.3) §2.3. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.86) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.revoke-old` | SHOULD · agent — When the User signs in again for more access, the new Account Token replaces the old one, which the Personal Agent revokes. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - If a task needs more access than the refresh token allows, the User signs in again, and the Personal Agent requests every scope it still needs, including previously granted scopes it wants to keep, following [RFC 6749 §3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.3). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.73) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.pending-unchanged` | MUST · company — A pending or failed sign-in leaves existing tokens unchanged. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A pending or failed sign-in leaves existing tokens unchanged. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.response` | MUST · company — Every sign-in type ends with the Account Token in refresh_token and a signed-in Session Token for the current Session, with scope unless the Company granted exactly the scopes requested, and at least one scope; a sign-in where the User approves none ends as a decline. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.61) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.one-account` | MUST · company — A Session is signed in to at most one account, and once it has been it can't switch to another, even after sign-out; the code exchange, device poll or Mediated Sign-In returns account_mismatch and issues no tokens. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Sign in with direct, device, or mediated sign-in, then join your browser to the session ([section 6](/docs/1.0/spec#web-browsing)). - Join the browser to the session right before you start browsing, and join again after signing in, or when the website stops recognizing the session. - A join without an access token leaves a login the browser already has, while a signed-in join replaces it, and the  | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.37) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.4 Signing in | `signin.token-snapshot` | MUST · company — Each Session Token reflects the Session as it was when issued, so a request with a signed-out token is handled as signed out and an account action fails with insufficient_scope. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A request without an access token is signed out, so an account action without one gets a challenge, and an access token without the needed scopes gets insufficient_scope (section 7). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.58) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.5 Direct Sign-In | `direct.bind-code` | MUST · company — The Company binds the code to the Session Key, and at the exchange checks that the proof's key is that key and that its Session belongs to the same client_id, returning invalid_grant for a proof from a different key. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Rate-limit attempts by account, by client_id, and by network address, and cap wrong one-time codes. - Require a one-time code when a sign-in looks unusual, including a personal agent's first mediated sign-in after the user disconnected it. - Return the same invalid_grant error for an unknown account and a wrong password, so responses don't reveal whether an account exists. - Never store raw  | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.36) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.5 Direct Sign-In | `direct.consent` | MUST · company — The Company's page shows which Personal Agent is asking, with the name and logo from its metadata, and the requested scopes, and lets the User approve some or all of them, or decline. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | A company whose accounts hold them, such as a bank, may offer only direct sign-in. - Follow the best practices for cross-device flows in [RFC 10027](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc10027) §2 (MUST, [4.3](/docs/1.0/spec#device-sign-in)): assess the risk before offering device sign-in, and choose mitigations from its §6.1, such as short-lived single-use codes, rate limits, limited scopes, and a co | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.5 Direct Sign-In | `direct.iss-check` | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent checks state, and checks that iss exactly matches the auth.issuer of the Company it started sign-in with. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST check that iss exactly matches the issuer of the Company it started sign-in with. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.73) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.5 Direct Sign-In | `direct.exchange` | MUST · company — At the code exchange the Company verifies the code, the PKCE verifier and the client assertion, and returns invalid_grant for an expired or reused code. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in | A wrong code returns code_required again, until the Company's attempt limit, and then invalid_grant. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.38) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.6 Device Sign-In | `device.polling` | MUST · company — Until the User finishes, the token endpoint returns authorization_pending or slow_down; then the tokens, access_denied if the User declined, or expired_token after expires_in. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Meanwhile the Personal Agent polls the token_endpoint every interval seconds until the User approves or declines, or the request expires, as RFC 8628 §3.4–3.5 describe. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.29) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.7 Mediated Sign-In | `mediated.transitional` | SHOULD · company — A Company offers Mediated Sign-In only until it can add Direct or Device Sign-In, and stops once those cover the scopes it grants. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in | A Company SHOULD offer Mediated Sign-In only until it can add Direct or Device Sign-In, and SHOULD stop offering it once those cover the scopes it grants. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.83) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.7 Mediated Sign-In | `mediated.secret-fields` | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent protects a value marked secret: never shown back to the User, written to logs, or sent anywhere else. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in | It MUST NOT be grant_type, scope, resource, a client authentication parameter such as client_id or client_assertion, or a parameter this extension defines, such as sign_in_id. - label is what to ask the User for. - secret marks a value the Personal Agent MUST protect: never shown back to the User, written to logs, or sent anywhere else. - scopes lists the scopes Mediated Sign-In  | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.87) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.7 Mediated Sign-In | `mediated.flow` | MUST · company — The endpoint answers each status with HTTP 200, a wrong code returns code_required until the Company's attempt limit and then failed, a code submitted to a failed or expired sign-in returns that status again, and only the Session that started a sign-in can finish it. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in | A wrong code returns code_required again, until the Company's attempt limit, and then invalid_grant. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.45) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.7 Mediated Sign-In | `mediated.no-credentials-returned` | MUST NOT · company — A Company that offers Mediated Sign-In does not return credentials in any response. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | When a company offers the [Mediated Sign-In extension](/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in): | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.57) |

| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.7 Mediated Sign-In | `mediated.endpoint-only` | MUST NOT · agent — Personal Agents send credentials only to the Company's auth.mediated.endpoint. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in | Personal Agents MUST NOT send credentials anywhere other than the token_endpoint of the Company's issuer, with this grant. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.75) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.7 Mediated Sign-In | `mediated.by-issuer` | MUST · agent — A Personal Agent chooses which stored credentials to send by the Company's auth.issuer, never by the field labels or the endpoint, and shows the Company's organization.name and domain whenever it asks the User for credentials or a code. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in | A Personal Agent MUST choose which stored credentials to send by the Company's issuer, never by the field labels or the endpoint, and MUST show the Company's organization.name and domain whenever it asks the User for credentials or a code. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.86) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.7 Mediated Sign-In | `mediated.discard` | MUST · agent — After a sign-in ends, the Personal Agent discards the credentials or keeps them only in a credential store the User chose, and nowhere else, including model context and logs. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in | **Storing credentials.** After a sign-in ends, with tokens or an error other than code_required, the Personal Agent MUST either discard the credentials or keep them only in a credential store the User chose, such as a password manager. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.56) |
