| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.8 Account Tokens | `account.where-accepted` | MUST · company — Companies accept an Account Token only at their token_endpoint and revocation_endpoint, and only from the client_id it was issued to, authenticated by its client assertion. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.70) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.8 Account Tokens | `account.session-match` | MUST · company — The Session belongs to the same client_id and User ID as the Account Token, and has not been signed in to a different account, even if now signed out; otherwise the Company returns invalid_grant or account_mismatch. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - The response describes what the Company offers, not the User's account, so the request carries no access token, even in a signed-in Session. - A Company MAY give different Sessions different responses, based on the Session, User ID, or Agent ID, for example to try a new skill with some Users. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.8 Account Tokens | `account.narrow` | MAY · agent — scope may ask for fewer scopes than the Account Token has. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It MAY send scope to ask for fewer scopes than the refresh token has, for example read-only access for a task that only looks things up, and resource for a token for one service (4.5). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.80) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.8 Account Tokens | `account.no-widen` | MUST NOT · company — A refresh can't ask for more scopes than the Account Token has. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - If a task needs more access than the refresh token allows, the User signs in again, and the Personal Agent requests every scope it still needs, including previously granted scopes it wants to keep, following [RFC 6749 §3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.3). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.80) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.8 Account Tokens | `account.lifetime` | MUST · company — Using an Account Token doesn't extend its lifetime, and an expired or revoked one returns invalid_grant while the Session continues signed out. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent finds out when a request fails with invalid_token, or refresh returns invalid_grant, and then continues signed out. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.43) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.8 Account Tokens | `account.rotation` | MAY · company — A Company may return a new Account Token in refresh_token. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | \| Poppy term \| OAuth term \| Why the Poppy name \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| User ID \| Pairwise subject identifier, sub in the Session JWT \| The Personal Agent assigns it, not the Company's OAuth server as in OpenID Connect (5.1). \| \| Sign-in \| User authorization \| It names the User's approval of the Personal Agent's access, whatever the flow (section 4). \| \| Sign-out \| Revoking the refresh token \| It | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.80) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.8 Account Tokens | `account.use-rotated` | MUST · agent — When the Company returns a new Account Token, the Personal Agent uses the new one from then on. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | \| Poppy term \| OAuth term \| Why the Poppy name \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| User ID \| Pairwise subject identifier, sub in the Session JWT \| The Personal Agent assigns it, not the Company's OAuth server as in OpenID Connect (5.1). \| \| Sign-in \| User authorization \| It names the User's approval of the Personal Agent's access, whatever the flow (section 4). \| \| Sign-out \| Revoking the refresh token \| It | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.71) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.8 Account Tokens | `account.not-key-bound` | MUST · company — The Company doesn't bind the Account Token to a Session Key, so one Account Token can start Sessions with different keys. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - The response describes what the Company offers, not the User's account, so the request carries no access token, even in a signed-in Session. - A Company MAY give different Sessions different responses, based on the Session, User ID, or Agent ID, for example to try a new skill with some Users. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.67) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.8 Account Tokens | `account.secret` | MUST · agent — Personal Agents store Account Tokens as secrets, and never put them in model context, conversation messages, logs or URLs. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Keep them out of logs, telemetry, error reports, model context, conversation messages, and URLs. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.46) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.9 Signing out | `signout.revoke` | MUST · company — The Company revokes the Account Token and signs out every Session signed in with it, and those Sessions continue signed out, able to sign in again only to the same account. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | When a refresh response omits it, keep the existing refresh token. - After the user signs in again for more scopes, revoke the old refresh token ([4.7](/docs/1.0/spec#scopes)). - On invalid_grant for a refresh token, continue signed out: send the Session header without an access token ([5.5](/docs/1.0/spec#ending-sessions)). - Use a new session for a second account ([5.4](/docs/1.0/spec#sign-in) | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.64) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.9 Signing out | `signout.end-sessions` | MAY · company — A Company may end the Sessions at sign-out instead, and renewal then returns invalid_grant. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company can also accept a Personal Agent for signed-out Sessions but refuse it sign-in, or one sign-in type, with the OAuth unauthorized_client error from the grant's endpoint. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.9 Signing out | `signout.invalidate-tokens` | SHOULD · company — The Company also invalidates the Session Tokens issued under the same sign-in, as RFC 7009 §2.1 describes. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | What happens to access tokens issued under a revoked refresh token, including one that a new sign-in replaced (4.7), follows [RFC 7009](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7009) §2.1, and the [guides](/docs/1.0/guides#company-lifetimes) suggest lifetimes. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.60) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.9 Signing out | `signout.check-per-request` | SHOULD · company — A Company that can't invalidate them checks on each request whether the Session is still signed in. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Fetching a page without a browser, such as a policy, is still fine, with the Session header ([5.2](/docs/1.0/spec#session-header)). - To browse signed in, request poppy:browser-sign-in with the other scopes the task needs, from a company that lists browser_session_endpoint ([section 6](/docs/1.0/spec#web-browsing)). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.63) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.9 Signing out | `signout.short-lifetimes` | SHOULD · company — A Company that does neither uses much shorter Session Token lifetimes, such as a few minutes. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | When a request uses Session identity or state, the Company verifies and joins the two: the Session header says which Session and User the request is for, and the access token says which account it can act on, with which scopes. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.44) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.9 Signing out | `signout.running-work` | MAY · company — Work the Company accepted with a valid token can continue after that token expires, and when access is revoked the Company may stop work in progress that uses the account, or let it finish. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | When a request uses Session identity or state, the Company verifies and joins the two: the Session header says which Session and User the request is for, and the access token says which account it can act on, with which scopes. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.33) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.9 Signing out | `signout.disconnect` | MUST · company — When the User disconnects the Personal Agent in account settings, the Company revokes its Account Tokens for that account and signs out its Sessions. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent can have several Sessions for the same User (section 5). \| \| Session header \| The Poppy-Session header on every request a Personal Agent sends to a Company's APIs and endpoints: a JWT the Personal Agent signs for that request, naming the Session and the User ID (5.2). \| \| Delegated key \| A short-lived key a Personal Agent signs its Session JWTs with. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.55) |
| spec/pap-0.3/04-sign-in.md | 4.9 Signing out | `signout.no-resign-in` | SHOULD NOT · agent — After sign-out, the Personal Agent does not sign in to that account again unless the User asks, even when a request gets insufficient_scope. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A conversation stays open, so the User can sign in again and continue it. - The Personal Agent SHOULD NOT sign in to that account again unless the User asks. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.57) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.page` | MUST · company — The endpoint answers a GET with an HTML page whose form has a hidden code and an empty proof field and posts to the endpoint. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - The form has a hidden field named code with a one-time code, and posts to the endpoint. - The Company MUST make the code unguessable and short-lived, and each code works once. - The Company MUST tie the code to the browser that loaded the page with a cookie set in the same response, so that only that browser can submit it. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.58) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.page-headers` | MUST · company — The page is sent with Cache-Control: no-store, Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none' and Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Send the page with Cache-Control: no-store, Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none', and Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin, so caches don't keep it, other pages can't frame it, and the page that opened it can't keep a reference to its window ([section 6](/docs/1.0/spec#web-browsing)). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.93) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.page-no-effect` | MUST NOT · company — Loading the page does not start, end or change any Session, or change the browser's other cookies. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company chooses its name and value. - The page MUST NOT be stored by caches, framed by other pages, or kept as a window reference by the page that opened it. - Loading the page MUST NOT start, end, or change any Session, or change the browser's other cookies. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.proof` | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent signs a DPoP Proof with the Session Key of the Session the browser joins, with htm POST, htu the endpoint, the code as nonce and no ath, puts it in the proof field and has its browser submit the form. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent authorizes the code.** The Personal Agent reads the code from the page in its browser and sends it to {browser_session_endpoint}/authorize, with the Session header of the Session the browser joins (5.2) and, if signed in, the access token the website should act with: | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.44) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.own-code` | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent takes the code only from the page its own browser loaded from the endpoint, never from anywhere else, and reads it and signs the proof in software its model can't read. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MUST take the code only from the page its own browser loaded from the endpoint in step 1, and MUST NOT authorize a code from anywhere else, such as a message, a conversation, or another page. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.53) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.verify` | MUST · company — The Company checks that the request has the cookie from the page and it matches code, that the code is one it issued, unexpired and unused, with a code used by the first request that sends it whatever the result, that proof is a valid DPoP Proof for this request with the code as nonce, and that its key is the Session Key of an active Session. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A code is used by the first request that submits it, whatever the result. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.29) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.response` | MUST · company — When every check passes, the Company sets its cookie for the Session, clears the page's cookie and responds 303 See Other to a page on its website; otherwise it sets no cookie and returns an error page with HTTP 400, or 429 for too many requests. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | If every check passes, the Company sets its cookie for that Session, clears the cookie from step 1, and redirects the browser to a page on its website. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.56) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.cookie-set-once` | MUST · company — The Session's cookie is set only in that response. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Tokens and Session JWTs MUST NOT be sent in a URL ([5.6](/docs/1.0/spec#sending-requests)), and tokens, Session JWTs, private keys, authorization codes, PKCE code verifiers, and device codes MUST NOT go in model context ([10.2](/docs/1.0/spec#secrets)). - Redact the Authorization, Poppy-Session, DPoP, and Cookie headers and credential fields before a request reaches a logger or tracer. - S | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.cookie-follows-session` | MUST · company — The cookie follows the Session's current state, so when the Session signs in or out or its scopes change, the website reflects it on the next request. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | When a request uses Session identity or state, the Company verifies and joins the two: the Session header says which Session and User the request is for, and the access token says which account it can act on, with which scopes. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.33) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.cookie-flags` | MUST · company — The cookie is Secure and HttpOnly. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Set the cookie that ties the code to the browser as __Host-poppy_join=…; Path=/; Max-Age=60; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict, with Max-Age no longer than the code's lifetime. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.cookie-host-prefix` | SHOULD · company — The cookie uses the __Host- prefix, unless it has to cover several hosts. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | The __Host- prefix keeps other hosts on your domain from setting it, and SameSite=Strict keeps a form on another site from sending it ([RFC 6265bis](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpbis-rfc6265bis/)). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.38) |
| spec/pap-0.3/05-web.md | 5 Web browsing | `web.scopes` | MUST · company — The website applies the Session's scopes, and while the browser carries the Session's cookie the Company handles its requests as that Session, whatever other cookies the browser holds. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | After a signed-in join, the Company handles them with the Session's account and scopes, whatever other cookies the browser holds from earlier visits. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.60) |
| spec/pap-0.3/06-apis.md | 6 APIs | `apis.types` | MUST · company — Each API's type is openapi or mcp. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | \| Type \| url points to \| Specification \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| openapi \| An OpenAPI 3.0 or 3.1 description of the API. \| [OpenAPI Specification 3.1](https://spec.openapis.org/oas/v3.1.1.html) \| \| mcp \| A remote MCP server endpoint, using the Streamable HTTP transport and MCP version 2025-06-18 or later. \| [Model Context Protocol 2025-06-18](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025- | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/06-apis.md | 6 APIs | `apis.openapi-description` | MUST · company — An openapi entry's url points to an OpenAPI 3.0 or 3.1 description of the API. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | \| Type \| Fields \| Points to \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| openapi \| url, operation_id \| An endpoint in an OpenAPI API. url is the url of an openapi entry in apis (section 7), and operation_id is the endpoint's operationId in that description. \| \| mcp \| url, tool \| A tool on an MCP server. url is the url of an mcp entry in apis, and tool is the tool's name. \| \| web \| | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/06-apis.md | 6 APIs | `apis.mcp-transport` | MUST · company — An mcp entry's url is a remote MCP server endpoint using the Streamable HTTP transport and MCP version 2025-06-18 or later. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | \| Type \| url points to \| Specification \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| openapi \| An OpenAPI 3.0 or 3.1 description of the API. \| [OpenAPI Specification 3.1](https://spec.openapis.org/oas/v3.1.1.html) \| \| mcp \| A remote MCP server endpoint, using the Streamable HTTP transport and MCP version 2025-06-18 or later. \| [Model Context Protocol 2025-06-18](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025- | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.91) |
| spec/pap-0.3/06-apis.md | 6 APIs | `apis.session-tokens` | MUST · company — Every listed API accepts Session Tokens as described in 4.3: DPoP for OpenAPI, and for MCP, DPoP if the server supports it and Bearer if it doesn't. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.45) |
| spec/pap-0.3/06-apis.md | 6 APIs | `apis.mcp-scopes` | MUST · company — A listed MCP server accepts Session Tokens as its access tokens and enforces their scopes. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Every listed API MUST accept the Session header (5.2) and, when the Company offers account access, its access tokens (4.5), and enforce their scopes. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.63) |
| spec/pap-0.3/06-apis.md | 6 APIs | `apis.mcp-metadata` | MUST · company — The MCP server's protected resource metadata (RFC 9728) lists auth.issuer in authorization_servers. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Domains are compared ignoring case, with no other normalization. \| \| auth \| No \| The Company's sign-in details: issuer, disabled, scopes, and scope_hints, below. \| \| auth.issuer \| If the domain's protected resource metadata lists more than one authorization server \| The Company's OAuth issuer identifier, naming the one Personal Agents use (3.2). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.80) |
| spec/pap-0.3/06-apis.md | 6 APIs | `apis.mcp-clients` | MAY · company — An MCP client that doesn't implement this protocol may sign in with MCP's own authorization flow, which starts a signed-in Session with no User ID that ends when its sign-in is revoked or expires. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.46) |
| spec/pap-0.3/06-apis.md | 6 APIs | `apis.errors` | MUST · company — Token failures at APIs return invalid_token (401), invalid_dpop_proof or use_dpop_nonce (401), insufficient_scope (403, naming the scopes in scope) or extension_required (403) in the WWW-Authenticate header. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Don't use an unverified iss, sub, or sid for attribution, rate limits by identity, cache keys, or state lookup. - Return invalid_session for a failed check, and keep access token errors in WWW-Authenticate, as OAuth does ([section 7](/docs/1.0/spec#apis)). - Binding access tokens with DPoP is your choice ([4.5](/docs/1.0/spec#access-tokens)), and you can choose for each service. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.38) |
| spec/pap-0.3/06-apis.md | 6 APIs | `apis.mcp-challenges` | MUST · company — An MCP server's 401 responses carry a challenge for each scheme it accepts, each with resource_metadata naming its protected resource metadata URL. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **HTTPS.** Every URL this protocol defines or exchanges MUST be an HTTPS URL: in poppy.json, in authorization server and protected resource metadata, in client metadata documents (4.1), in extensions, and in assertions, Session JWTs, and responses. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.1 Conversation protocols | `conv.protocol-choice` | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent chooses a protocol it supports from agent.protocols, uses that entry's endpoint, and skips unfamiliar types. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent chooses a protocol it supports and uses that entry's endpoint for the conversation. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.67) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.2 Requests | `conv.session-token` | MUST · both — Every conversation request carries a Session Token with a DPoP Proof. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It uses the same characters as Conversation IDs (8.2). - sub is the User ID for this Company (5.1). - agent_id is the optional Agent ID (5.1). - htm and htu are the request's method and URL, without the query or fragment, as in a DPoP proof ([RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) §4.2). - iat is when the Personal Agent signed it, in Unix seconds, and jti is unique and ungue | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.75) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.2 Requests | `conv.isolation` | MUST · company — A conversation belongs to the client_id and User ID that started it, and to the account once it uses one; the Company rejects requests from any other client_id, User or account. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A conversation belongs to the Personal Agent's client_id and the User ID that started it, and, once it uses the User's account, to that account too. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.67) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.2 Requests | `conv.id-format` | MUST · both — Conversation, message and event IDs are strings of 1 to 256 characters from the URL-safe base64 alphabet. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **IDs.** Conversation IDs, request IDs, message IDs, and event IDs are strings of 1 to 256 characters from the URL-safe base64 alphabet ([RFC 4648, section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4648#section-5)): letters, digits, -, and _. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.2 Requests | `conv.id-prefix` | SHOULD · both — IDs start with a short prefix for their kind, such as cnv_, msg_ or evt_. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Personal Agent Protocol (Poppy for short) defines how a Personal Agent, acting for one User, works with a Company on that User's behalf. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.25) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.2 Requests | `conv.id-opaque` | MUST NOT · both — Beyond the format rules, the side that doesn't choose an ID does not depend on its format. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Beyond these rules, the side that chooses an ID decides its format, and the other side MUST NOT depend on it. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.83) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.3 Sending messages | `conv.start` | MUST · company — Starting a conversation returns HTTP 201 with conversation_id, status and responder, and later messages to {id}/messages return HTTP 202 with the same fields. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Later messages go to the conversation's messages URL, which returns HTTP 202 with the same body: | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.55) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.3 Sending messages | `conv.message-id-unique` | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent makes each message id unique for the User, and retries a message with the same ID. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | It uses the same characters as Conversation IDs (8.2). - sub is the User ID for this Company (5.1). - agent_id is the optional Agent ID (5.1). - htm and htu are the request's method and URL, without the query or fragment, as in a DPoP proof ([RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) §4.2). - iat is when the Personal Agent signed it, in Unix seconds, and jti is unique and ungue | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.63) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.3 Sending messages | `conv.message-idempotent` | MUST · company — A message with an already accepted ID and the same content returns the original response without adding it again, including a retried first message; different content returns message_id_conflict. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | If the Company has already accepted a request with that ID, path, and body, it returns the original response and doesn't act on the request again. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.38) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.3 Sending messages | `conv.wait` | MUST · company — With the wait query parameter (and optionally cursor) on either POST, the Company accepts the message, then responds as a read of the events from that cursor would, holding the request up to wait seconds, with status 201 or 202. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company accepts the request, then responds as a read of the conversation's events from that cursor would (8.5), with the conversation's state, holding the request for up to wait seconds. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.61) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.4 Message fields | `conv.message-content` | MUST · both — A message has at least one of text, data or context. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A message MUST have at least one of text, data, context, or extensions. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.4 Message fields | `conv.text-plain` | SHOULD NOT · both — Personal Agents and Company Agents do not put JSON or other markup in text. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Let your company agent do everything customers can do by phone, as fully as you can, so personal agents have no reason to call. - Put structured details in data, not JSON or other markup in text ([8.4](/docs/1.0/spec#message-fields)). - To a personal agent that declares the [Links extension](/docs/1.0/extensions/links), send the pages a reply refers to as links, rather than URLs in text. - | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.4 Message fields | `conv.context-formats` | MUST · agent — When the Personal Agent shares locale, time_zone or user_available, it uses those fields and their formats: an RFC 5646 language tag, an IANA time zone name, and a boolean. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - locale is a language tag such as en-US ([RFC 5646](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5646)), for the language and formatting of replies. - time_zone is an IANA time zone name such as America/Los_Angeles, for reading and showing times. - user_available is true if the User can answer questions right now. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.56) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.4 Message fields | `conv.context-extra` | MAY · agent — context may include other fields. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | context MAY include other fields, such as accessibility needs or preferred units. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.4 Message fields | `conv.context-ignore` | MAY · company — A Company may ignore context fields beyond those defined. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | From 1.0, a newer minor version won't break older Personal Agents, which use the document and ignore fields they don't recognize. \| \| supported_versions \| No \| The other protocol versions the Company serves, each mapped to the URL of that version's document (3.4). \| \| organization \| Yes \| The Company's display name, its domain, and an optional logo_uri, an HTTPS URL of the Company's logo | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.4 Message fields | `conv.context-minimal` | SHOULD · agent — Personal Agents share only what the task needs. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Limit unverified requests by network instead. - Companies: also send the RateLimit and RateLimit-Policy headers ([IETF draft](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers/)), so personal agents can slow down before they hit a limit. - Personal agents: keep track of limits for each company and share them across your tasks and users, rather than letting each task find th | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.83) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.4 Message fields | `conv.role` | MUST · company — The Company sets each recorded message's role to user or company for the side it came from, and ignores a role the Personal Agent sends. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company sets it when it records the message as an event (8.5): the request's ID for the Personal Agent's messages (8.3), or an ID it chooses for its own. \| \| role \| No \| user or company, the side the message came from. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.75) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.4 Message fields | `conv.author-details` | MAY · company — A Company whose messages come from several people or AI personas may name the writer of each in author: a stable id, a display name and an HTTPS image URL, each optional. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - type is required: agent or human, as 8.8 describes. - id is a stable ID for the author, chosen by the Company, so the Personal Agent can tell the same author's messages apart from others'. - name is the author's display name, such as Alex. - image is the URL of the author's picture, for the Personal Agent to show next to the name. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.47) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.4 Message fields | `conv.author-company-only` | MUST · company — Only Company messages carry author.id, name and image; from a Personal Agent's message the Company records only author.type. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - type is required: agent or human, as 8.8 describes. - id is a stable ID for the author, chosen by the Company, so the Personal Agent can tell the same author's messages apart from others'. - name is the author's display name, such as Alex. - image is the URL of the author's picture, for the Personal Agent to show next to the name. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.73) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.4 Message fields | `conv.context-sticky` | MUST · company — context fields a message leaves out keep their previous values, and only the Personal Agent sends context. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Any message can carry context, and fields it leaves out keep their previous values. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.70) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.5 Reading events | `conv.skip-unknown-events` | MUST · agent — Personal Agents skip event types they don't recognize. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Personal Agents MUST skip event types they don't recognize, so later versions and extensions can add types. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.5 Reading events | `conv.cursor-opaque` | MUST NOT · agent — Cursors are opaque, and the Personal Agent does not compare or sort them. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Cursors are opaque, so the Personal Agent MUST NOT compare or sort them. - wait is a whole number of seconds, 0 or more. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.5 Reading events | `conv.events` | MUST · company — Each event's id is also a cursor, a read without cursor starts at the beginning, a read that times out returns an empty events list, and every message the Company accepts is recorded as it was sent, including one with only context. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A read that times out returns an empty events list. - Every successful read MUST include conversation_id, events, cursor, has_more, and the state fields working, handoff, responder, and closed (8.7), with events empty when there are none. cursor is the last event returned, or the request's cursor if there were none. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.47) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.5 Reading events | `conv.wait-cap` | MAY · company — The Company may return a read before wait elapses, or cap wait. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Read events with a long wait, such as 30 seconds, or a stream, rather than many short polls ([8.5](/docs/1.0/spec#reading-events)). - Read until working is false before treating the company's reply as complete ([8.7](/docs/1.0/spec#state)). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.67) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.5 Reading events | `conv.wait-cap-held` | MUST · company — A wait larger than the Company's cap is held for the cap, not rejected, and wait is a whole number of seconds, 0 or more. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Cursors are opaque, so the Personal Agent MUST NOT compare or sort them. - wait is a whole number of seconds, 0 or more. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.56) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.5 Reading events | `conv.read-fields` | MUST · company — Every successful read includes conversation_id, events, cursor, has_more, status and responder, with events empty when there are none; cursor is the last event returned, or the request's cursor if there were none. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A read that times out returns an empty events list. - Every successful read MUST include conversation_id, events, cursor, has_more, and the state fields working, handoff, responder, and closed (8.7), with events empty when there are none. cursor is the last event returned, or the request's cursor if there were none. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.88) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.5 Reading events | `conv.cursor-expired-error` | MUST · company — A cursor for events the Company no longer has returns cursor_expired. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A cursor for events it no longer has returns cursor_expired. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.83) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.5 Reading events | `conv.cursor-expired` | SHOULD · agent — On cursor_expired the Personal Agent reads from the beginning and tells the User that part of the conversation is missing. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Messages from people carry neither. - On cursor_expired, tell the user that part of the conversation is missing ([8.5](/docs/1.0/spec#reading-events)). - Show text-delta pieces only as a preview. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.55) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.6 Streaming | `conv.sse` | MAY · company — A Company may stream events with server-sent events when a read sends Accept: text/event-stream, with each event's ID as the SSE id and the event JSON as data; one that doesn't stream returns JSON. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company MAY stream events with server-sent events ([SSE](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/server-sent-events.html)). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.38) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.6 Streaming | `conv.sse-close` | MAY · company — A Company may close a stream at any time, and the Personal Agent reconnects with Last-Event-ID. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company sets it when it records the message as an event (8.5). \| \| as_of_request \| No \| On a message from the Company Agent, the ID of the latest request it took into account (below). \| \| as_of_cursor \| No \| On a message the Personal Agent wrote, the last cursor it had read (below). \| \| author \| Yes \| Who wrote the message. type is agent if the Personal Agent wrote it, human if the | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.56) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.6 Streaming | `conv.last-event-id` | MUST · company — When a request has both Last-Event-ID and cursor, Last-Event-ID wins. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | If a request has both, Last-Event-ID wins, because SSE clients add it to the URL the stream was first opened with. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.83) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.6 Streaming | `conv.text-delta` | MAY · company — While the Company Agent writes a message, the Company may send pieces of its text as text-delta events with no SSE id. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | While the Company Agent writes a message, the Company MAY send pieces of its text as text-delta events, with no SSE id: | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.6 Streaming | `conv.text-delta-ignore` | MUST NOT · agent — The Personal Agent does not act on text from a text-delta event. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent asks for a stream by sending Accept: text/event-stream on a read. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.80) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.7 Status and responder | `conv.status-responder` | MUST · company — Every successful response to a POST or a read includes status and responder. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A read that times out returns an empty events list. - Every successful read MUST include conversation_id, events, cursor, has_more, and the state fields working, handoff, responder, and closed (8.7), with events empty when there are none. cursor is the last event returned, or the request's cursor if there were none. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.7 Status and responder | `conv.state-events` | MUST · company — Every change to status or responder adds a state event with both. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Every change to handoff, responder, or closed adds a state event with all three, even if there is no new text. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.75) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.7 Status and responder | `conv.company-sender` | MUST · company — Each Company message includes author.type, agent or human, and its role stays company. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Company sets it when it records the message as an event (8.5). \| \| as_of_request \| No \| On a message from the Company Agent, the ID of the latest request it took into account (below). \| \| as_of_cursor \| No \| On a message the Personal Agent wrote, the last cursor it had read (below). \| \| author \| Yes \| Who wrote the message. type is agent if the Personal Agent wrote it, human if the | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.67) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.8 Human or AI | `conv.agent-sender` | MUST · agent — The Personal Agent sets each message's author.type to agent when it wrote the message, and to human only when the message carries the User's own words. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | On the Personal Agent's side, the Personal Agent MUST set each message's author.type: agent when the Personal Agent wrote it, and human only when it carries the User's own words. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.91) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.9 Handoff to a person | `conv.handoff` | MUST · company — A handoff request with an empty JSON object returns HTTP 202 with status and responder; the status becomes queued, responder becomes human when a person joins, and if no one is available the status returns to idle with a message saying why; a handoff to a conversation already queued or with a person returns HTTP 202 with the current state and changes nothing. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A read that times out returns an empty events list. - Every successful read MUST include conversation_id, events, cursor, has_more, and the state fields working, handoff, responder, and closed (8.7), with events empty when there are none. cursor is the last event returned, or the request's cursor if there were none. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.29) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.10 Direct Conversations | `conv.direct` | MUST · company — The parent of a Direct Conversation is open, belongs to the same owner, is not itself a Direct Conversation, and has at most one open Direct Conversation; otherwise the Company returns conversation_not_found, conversation_closed or direct_conversation_open. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Otherwise the Company returns conversation_not_found for an unknown parent or one with a different owner, conversation_closed for a closed one, invalid_request for one that is a Direct Conversation, and direct_conversation_open for one that already has an open Direct Conversation. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.71) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.10 Direct Conversations | `conv.direct-parent` | MUST · company — Opening and closing a Direct Conversation adds direct_opened and direct_closed events to the parent, the parent refuses messages and handoff with direct_conversation_open while it is open, and closing the parent closes its open Direct Conversation. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | While it is open, the parent can still be read, but messages, handoff requests, and handoff cancels to the parent return direct_conversation_open, with the Direct Conversation's ID in conversation_id. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.47) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.10 Direct Conversations | `conv.user-requested` | MUST · company — A user_requested event, with a short reason, doesn't change status or responder. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | **When the Company asks.** The Company asks to talk with the User by adding a user_requested event to the conversation, with a short reason: | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.63) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.11 Sign-in during a conversation | `conv.authorization-message` | SHOULD · company — When the Company adds an authorization event, it also asks in a message. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Merely switching away from the direct conversation needn't close it. - If a company agent asks in a message for a one-time code, or for details to verify the user, such as a date of birth or part of an account number, ask it to request sign-in with an authorization event instead ([8.11](/docs/1.0/spec#conversation-sign-in)). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.83) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.11 Sign-in during a conversation | `conv.token-switch` | MAY · agent — The Personal Agent may switch to a new Session Token partway through a conversation, or continue it in a new Session, as long as the token matches the conversation's owner. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The Personal Agent MAY start or stop sending an access token partway through a conversation, after signing in, refreshing, or signing out, or continue it in a new Session, as long as the request matches the conversation's owner (8.2). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.92) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.11 Sign-in during a conversation | `conv.scopes` | MUST · company — Before reading account information or making a change, the Company Agent checks for the required scopes, whoever is handling the conversation, and sending a message does not authorize an account action. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - changes is none if no action changes the account or anything else, reversible if every change can be undone, such as editing a saved address, and irreversible if any change can't be undone, such as cancelling an order, deleting data, sending a message, or paying. - personal_data is true if any action shows or changes the User's personal data, such as contact details, addresses, or pa | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.28) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.11 Sign-in during a conversation | `conv.authorization-event` | MUST · company — When the Company Agent needs the User's account or more scopes, the Company adds an authorization event with error insufficient_scope and the needed scope, and the conversation stays open. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | See 8.7 for their values. \| \| authorization \| error and scope: the Company needs sign-in or more scopes (8.11). \| \| user_requested \| reason: the Company asks to talk with the User directly (8.10). \| \| direct_opened, direct_closed \| conversation_id: a Direct Conversation linked to this one opened or closed (8.10). \| | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.53) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.11 Sign-in during a conversation | `conv.account-bound` | MUST · company — Once a conversation has used the User's account, a request on it from a token for the same User that isn't signed in to that account gets insufficient_scope, and after a sign-out the conversation stays open. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Once a conversation has used the User's account, a request on it for the same User without an access token for that account gets a challenge, as an account action does (section 7). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.56) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.12 Closing | `conv.close` | MUST · company — Closing with an empty JSON object adds a state event with status: "closed", cancels any pending handoff and returns HTTP 200 with status and responder; closing a closed conversation returns the same response and adds no event; a closed conversation takes no more messages and its events stay readable. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A read that times out returns an empty events list. - Every successful read MUST include conversation_id, events, cursor, has_more, and the state fields working, handoff, responder, and closed (8.7), with events empty when there are none. cursor is the last event returned, or the request's cursor if there were none. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.42) |
| spec/pap-0.3/07-conversations.md | 7.13 Errors | `conv.errors` | MUST · company — Non-token errors have a JSON body with an error code and the statuses above, and an unknown Conversation ID and one belonging to someone else return the same conversation_not_found. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Keep error_description and other error text free of account details and other users' data. - Return the same error for "doesn't exist" and "belongs to someone else", as conversation_not_found does ([8.13](/docs/1.0/spec#conversation-errors)). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.1 Excluded tasks | `tasks.document` | MUST · company — excluded_tasks.url serves a JSON document with tasks, each with a task phrase of at most 200 characters and an optional alternative. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | \| Field \| Req. \| Description \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| task \| Yes \| The task, in a short phrase, such as "Send a wire transfer" or "Close an account". \| \| alternative \| No \| Another way to do the task, such as in the Company's app or on its website, or a different task that serves the same need (9.2). \| | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.36) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.1 Excluded tasks | `tasks.public` | MUST · company — The document is the same for every User, and is fetched with a GET and no token. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Its path can be anything, unlike poppy.json's (section 3), because poppy.json names it. - Every document a Company serves at the same time MUST have the same organization.domain and select the same issuer (3.2), so that a new version doesn't change the Company's identifier, and with it User IDs and refresh tokens. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.67) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.1 Excluded tasks | `tasks.cache` | SHOULD · agent — The Personal Agent caches the document according to its HTTP caching headers. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Check the address you actually connect to, not only the one DNS returned first. - Cache according to HTTP caching headers, within bounds you set, such as at least a minute and at most a day. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.1 Excluded tasks | `tasks.read-first` | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent reads the document before it starts on a task at the Company. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company that gives every Session the same response can serve it without verifying the header. - A Company that gives different Sessions different responses MUST mark them private or no-store in Cache-Control ([RFC 9111](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9111) §5.2.2), so that a shared cache doesn't give one Session's response to another. - A Personal Agent SHOULD get the response before it | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.75) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.1 Excluded tasks | `tasks.skip-unknown` | MUST · agent — Personal Agents skip fields they don't recognize. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | From 1.0, a newer minor version won't break older Personal Agents, which use the document and ignore fields they don't recognize. \| \| supported_versions \| No \| The other protocol versions the Company serves, each mapped to the URL of that version's document (3.4). \| \| organization \| Yes \| The Company's display name, its domain, and an optional logo_uri, an HTTPS URL of the Company's logo | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.80) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.2 Alternatives | `tasks.alternative-is-content` | MUST · agent — An alternative is content from the Company, and its instructions are not instructions the Personal Agent has to follow. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Acting at another Company uses that Company's discovery, Session, and any needed account authorization. - Personal Agents MUST skip reference types they don't recognize, so later versions can add types, such as the Company Agent or an extension. - An alternative is content from the Company (10.1). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.71) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.3 Feedback | `feedback.send` | MAY · agent — When a User's task can't be done through the protocol, the Personal Agent may send a report to feedback.endpoint. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Defaults to none. \| \| skill_index \| No \| An Agent Skills discovery index of the skills the Company publishes (9.4). \| \| feedback \| No \| endpoint is an HTTPS URL where Personal Agents report a User's task that can't be done (9.3). \| | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.73) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.3 Feedback | `feedback.session-token` | MUST · company — A Company accepts a report only with a Session Token, signed in or signed out, and needs no scope for it. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - A Company SHOULD verify the Session header on a report, signed in or signed out, and needs no scope for it. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.75) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.3 Feedback | `feedback.kind` | MUST · agent — Personal Agents send only the kind missing, with a task of at most 200 characters and a description of at most 2,000 characters. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - The Company SHOULD describe each scope it offers Personal Agents, so the Personal Agent can choose the ones a task needs: in the scopes of an OAuth 2.0 security scheme in an OpenAPI description it lists (section 7), or otherwise in auth.scopes. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.44) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.3 Feedback | `feedback.no-personal-data` | SHOULD NOT · agent — Personal Agents don't put personal data in a report, such as the User's name, contact details or order details. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | The report doesn't repeat these identities or say where the problem happened. - Personal Agents SHOULD NOT put personal data in a report, such as the User's name, contact details, or order details. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.3 Feedback | `feedback.no-secrets` | MUST NOT · agent — Personal Agents don't put secrets in a report. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Personal Agents MUST NOT put secrets in a report (10.2). - A Personal Agent SHOULD send one report for each task it couldn't do, not one for each attempt. - A Personal Agent SHOULD NOT report a task the Company lists as excluded (9.1), since the Company already knows it doesn't offer it. - A report can be wrong, or written to mislead. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.3 Feedback | `feedback.once` | SHOULD · agent — A Personal Agent sends one report for each task it couldn't do, not one for each attempt. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Personal Agents MUST NOT put secrets in a report (10.2). - A Personal Agent SHOULD send one report for each task it couldn't do, not one for each attempt. - A Personal Agent SHOULD NOT report a task the Company lists as excluded (9.1), since the Company already knows it doesn't offer it. - A report can be wrong, or written to mislead. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.86) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.3 Feedback | `feedback.not-excluded` | SHOULD NOT · agent — A Personal Agent does not report a task the Company lists as excluded. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Personal Agents MUST NOT put secrets in a report (10.2). - A Personal Agent SHOULD send one report for each task it couldn't do, not one for each attempt. - A Personal Agent SHOULD NOT report a task the Company lists as excluded (9.1), since the Company already knows it doesn't offer it. - A report can be wrong, or written to mislead. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.3 Feedback | `feedback.unknown-kind` | MUST · company — A Company accepts a kind it doesn't recognize. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Operator recognition alone does not verify Session claims, authorize account access, or decide whether the Company accepts the request. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/08-tasks.md | 8.3 Feedback | `feedback.as-data` | MUST · company — Companies treat a report's text as data, not as instructions, including when a model reads it. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Companies MUST treat a report's text as data, not as instructions, including when a model reads it. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |

| spec/pap-0.3/09-security.md | 9.2 Secrets and the model | `security.secrets-out-of-model` | MUST · agent — Personal Agents keep Session Tokens, Account Tokens, private keys, authorization codes, PKCE code verifiers and device codes out of model context. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Following [draft-ietf-wimse-aims](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-wimse-aims/) §8, which keeps an agent's credentials out of the model's reach, Personal Agents MUST keep these out of model context: access tokens (4.5), refresh tokens (4.4), Session JWTs (5.2), private keys, authorization codes and PKCE code verifiers (4.2), and device codes (4.3). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.94) |

| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.3.2 Who can use an operation | `ops.access` | MUST · company — An operation proposed signed out can be used only in its Session; one proposed signed in can be used by any Session of the same Personal Agent and User while it is signed in to the same account at the time of the request, a signed-out Session of that Personal Agent and User gets insufficient_scope, and anyone else gets operation_not_found. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/ucp | \| Request \| What the Personal Agent sends \| What it gets \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| Signed out \| The Session header; no access token \| What the Company offers without an account, as its UCP profile and access policy allow: catalog search and lookup, policies, Ask, carts, and guest checkout \| \| Signed in \| The Session header and an access token ([spec 4.5](/docs/1.0/spec#access-tokens)) \| The same, pe | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.53) |

| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.4 Proposing an operation | `ops.website-same-operation` | MUST · company — Confirming an operation on the website and at the endpoint can't perform it twice. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | \| Type \| Fields \| Points to \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| openapi \| url, operation_id \| An endpoint in an OpenAPI API. url is the url of an openapi entry in apis (section 7), and operation_id is the endpoint's operationId in that description. \| \| mcp \| url, tool \| A tool on an MCP server. url is the url of an mcp entry in apis, and tool is the tool's name. \| \| web \| | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.5.1 Getting the User's approval | `ops.approval` | MUST · agent — Before confirming, the Personal Agent has the User's approval for the revision, by the User (approved_by: "user") or by a standing permission, which is not allowed when user_approval_required is true. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | It shows what the personal agent stated the user approved ([Operations 8.1](/docs/1.0/extensions/operations#approval-proof)). - To ask for approval on your website, make the reference in Poppy-Operation a sealed copy of the operation and the request's fingerprint, such as a JWS or an HMAC over who can approve it as for the revision, so you don't store operations nobody approved ([Operations 4] | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.54) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.5.1 Getting the User's approval | `ops.company-text-not-approval` | MUST NOT · agent — The Personal Agent does not treat text from the Company, such as a conversation message, as the User's approval. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | It MUST NOT treat text from the Company, such as a conversation message, as the User's approval. - The Personal Agent can describe the terms itself, or show the values in terms. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.5.1 Getting the User's approval | `ops.show-terms` | SHOULD · agent — When the revision has terms, the Personal Agent shows the User the values that matter next to the summary, or checks that they agree with it, before asking for approval. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | The Personal Agent shows the User the terms: the summary, a description it writes from terms, or the values in terms themselves (5). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.47) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.5.1 Getting the User's approval | `ops.standing-permission` | MUST · agent — A standing permission covers the Company, the account, the action and every term of the revision, and is still in effect. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | The consequences of one action still come from the action itself, such as an MCP tool's annotations, or the HTTP method: a GET doesn't change anything. - Ask for the fewest scopes the task needs, not every scope the company offers. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.36) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.5.1 Getting the User's approval | `ops.approval-per-revision` | MUST · agent — An approval covers one revision, so a new revision needs a new approval unless a standing permission covers it. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/browser-control | \| \| Operations \| Browser control \| \| --- \| --- \| --- \| \| Who takes the step \| The Personal Agent, once the terms are approved \| The User, in the Personal Agent's browser \| \| What the User does \| Approves the terms in the Personal Agent's own interface, or gives the Personal Agent an instruction that covers them \| Takes over the browser and uses the Company's page \| \| Interfaces \| APIs, MCP servers | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.43) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.5.2 Confirming | `ops.confirm-checks` | MUST · company — Before performing the action, the Company checks that the Session Token may use the operation, that it has the needed scopes (insufficient_scope), that revision is the latest (terms_changed with the current operation), and that approved_by is allowed (user_approval_required). | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | One record can go from approved to performing to performed ([Operations 6](/docs/1.0/extensions/operations#sending-approval)). - When the browser repeats the request, check it against each unused approval recorded for that account or user as you would check a request that carries the approval: compute the terms and the HMAC again with the approved revision's operation_id and expiry. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.32) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.5.2 Confirming | `ops.confirm-idempotent` | MUST · company — Confirming an operation that is not proposed or has expired returns the operation as it is and does nothing else. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/mediated-sign-in | If the credentials are correct and nothing else is needed, the Company returns the token response in [spec 4.7](/docs/1.0/spec#scopes). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.43) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.5.2 Confirming | `ops.confirm-progress` | MUST · company — A confirmed operation moves to in_progress; the response carries the final state if the action finishes within a few seconds, or in_progress with Retry-After, and it continues even if the Session Token expires or the User signs out. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - The response describes what the Company offers, not the User's account, so the request carries no access token, even in a signed-in Session. - A Company MAY give different Sessions different responses, based on the Session, User ID, or Agent ID, for example to try a new skill with some Users. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.29) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Tracking and recovery | `ops.retry-after` | SHOULD · both — While an operation is in_progress, the Company includes Retry-After when it is read, and the Personal Agent reads again after that time. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | A new operation in answer to a retry doesn't say, so the Personal Agent finds out another way, such as from the order in the Company's API. - On a website, if the browser's request fails or times out after the approval was recorded, the Personal Agent has the browser make it again. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.54) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Tracking and recovery | `ops.failed-only-if-known` | MUST NOT · company — The Company does not report failed while it doesn't know whether the action took effect. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | **Looking up an approved operation.** Once an operation has a recorded approval, a request to endpoint with its reference and no approval responds with HTTP 200 and the operation's state in status: approved while the approval waits for the browser's request, performing while the Company performs the action or doesn't yet know whether it took effect, and performed after. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.78) |
