| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.6 Tracking and recovery | `ops.result-summary` | MUST · company — In a final state, result.summary describes every change that was made, including any made by an operation that failed or was cancelled partway. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Every message the Company accepts is recorded, including one with only context. \| \| state \| handoff, responder, and closed, after any of them changes. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.23) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.7 Cancelling | `ops.cancel` | MUST · company — Cancelling makes a proposed operation cancelled, stops an in_progress one if the Company can while it stays in_progress until the outcome is known, and returns a final operation as it is. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | - The Company MUST perform each operation at most once, however many times its approval is sent. - A request with the approval of an operation already performed gets the response the Company gave the first time, or one that says the same, for as long as the Company remembers the operation. - The Company MUST remember that it performed an operation at least until the approved revision's expires_at | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.27) |
| spec/pap-0.3/ext-operations.md | ops.9 Errors | `ops.errors` | MUST · company — The operations endpoint returns JSON errors: operation_not_found 404, terms_changed 409 with the current operation, user_approval_required 403, and rate_limited 429 with Retry-After. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/browser-control | - **Steps that are the User's to perform, not to approve.** The Company wants the User to make the choice on its page, such as picking from options the page shows, rather than approving a choice the Personal Agent made. - **Input the Personal Agent shouldn't handle.** The User types something into the page, such as passport details at check-in, so the Personal Agent doesn't need to collect it. - * | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.35) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.agents For personal agents | `guide.dpop-keys` | SHOULD · agent — Generate a new non-extractable Session Key for each Session and keep it as long as the Session, keep private keys away from models and their tools, sign proofs where the final method and URL are known, sign with ES256, and retry once on use_dpop_nonce. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Access tokens and refresh tokens. - Private keys, client assertions, Session JWTs, and DPoP proofs. - Authorization codes, PKCE code verifiers, device codes, one-time sign-in codes, browser-session codes, and credentials sent in mediated sign-in. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.29) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.client-fetch` | SHOULD · company — Fetch the client_id URL without following redirects, cache client metadata and key sets, keep allowlists or blocklists, and rate-limit Session creation and new client_id values. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Its client_id MUST equal the URL you fetched ([4.1](/docs/1.0/spec#agent-identity)). - Cache client metadata and key sets, as described in [Fetching metadata safely](#both-fetching). - Keep allowlists or blocklists of client_id values, and rate-limit session creation and new client_id values ([4.1](/docs/1.0/spec#agent-identity)). - Check whether your authorization server supports client ID  | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.75) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.dpop-header` | SHOULD · company — Check that a proof's typ is dpop+jwt, its alg is ES256 or one listed in dpop_signing_alg_values_supported, and its jwk holds only a public key. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Personal Agent that supports it sends a DPoP proof with its token requests when the issuer's metadata lists dpop_signing_alg_values_supported ([RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) §5.1), so the Company can bind the tokens. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.replay-cache` | SHOULD · company — Keep a replay cache keyed by jti for the acceptance window, shared by every server behind the token endpoint, and ideally by the APIs and conversation endpoint too. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Conversation requests already are, through their IDs ([8.3](/docs/1.0/spec#sending-messages)). - If you keep a replay cache keyed by jti, hold entries for the acceptance window and share it across every server. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.60) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.public-htu` | SHOULD · company — Compare htu with the public URL the Personal Agent used, built from the configured public origin rather than the received Host header. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Most requests then need one signature check. - Behind proxies and load balancers, compare htu with the public URL the personal agent used. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.42) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.dpop-window` | SHOULD · company — Use an acceptance window of about one minute plus a small clock skew. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Build it from your configured public origin, not from the Host header your server receives. - Use an acceptance window of about one minute for iat, plus a small clock skew. - Rejecting a reused jti is optional, as it is for DPoP proofs, because servers behind one endpoint don't always share state. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.mcp-dpop` | SHOULD · company — Support DPoP on a new MCP server, listing ES256 in its protected resource metadata, and issue Personal Agents only DPoP tokens for it. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | Domains are compared ignoring case, with no other normalization. \| \| auth \| No \| The Company's sign-in details: issuer, disabled, scopes, and scope_hints, below. \| \| auth.issuer \| If the domain's protected resource metadata lists more than one authorization server \| The Company's OAuth issuer identifier, naming the one Personal Agents use (3.2). | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.50) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.conversation-endpoint-only` | SHOULD · company — Serve Personal Agent conversations only from the conversation endpoint. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | An entry's description says, in plain language, what the agent at that endpoint can help with, so the Personal Agent can decide whether a conversation can serve the task at hand, and choose between the entries and the Company's APIs. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.83) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.session-start` | SHOULD · company — Handle the JWT bearer grant with an extension to the authorization server, a token service in front of the token endpoint, or user records created on first sight, kept apart from customer accounts and deleted when unused. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.29) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.client-metadata` | SHOULD · company — Check whether the authorization server supports client ID metadata documents, and if it needs registration, register the Personal Agents it accepts by client_id and reject others with invalid_client. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | If yours needs registration, register the personal agents you accept by their client_id URL and reject others with invalid_client, which 4.1 allows. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.53) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.session-keys` | SHOULD · company — Key Session records by the thumbprint of the key in each proof, remember the keys of ended Sessions at least as long as the longest Session lifetime, and don't bind Account Tokens to a Session Key. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | A Company MAY remember the sids of Sessions it ended, for as long as it chooses, so an ended Session doesn't silently start again. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.33) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.token-lifetimes` | SHOULD · company — Give Account Tokens a lifetime that fits the account, such as 90 days, expire unused ones sooner, revoke them on a password reset or suspected compromise, send refresh_token_expires_in when an Account Token has a lifetime, and don't stop accepted work because a Session Token expired. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | A job can outlast the token. - Where work in progress can take consequential actions on the user's account, such as payments or changes that are hard to undo, tie it to the refresh token, so revoking access stops it right away. - Give refresh tokens a lifetime that fits the account, such as 90 days, and expire ones that go unused sooner. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.52) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.revoke-stops-work` | SHOULD · company — Tie work in progress that can take consequential actions to the Account Token, so revoking access stops it right away. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | A job can outlast the token. - Where work in progress can take consequential actions on the user's account, such as payments or changes that are hard to undo, tie it to the refresh token, so revoking access stops it right away. - Give refresh tokens a lifetime that fits the account, such as 90 days, and expire ones that go unused sooner. | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.scope-hints` | SHOULD · company — Give each scope hints in auth.scope_hints, judged by the most consequential operation that requires it, leave a scope out rather than guess, and split a scope when that earns part of it a milder hint. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | When you're unsure about a scope, leave it out of auth.scope_hints rather than guess. - Splitting a scope can earn it a better hint. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.41) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.describing-apis` | SHOULD · company — Write API and MCP tool descriptions for a language model: say what each operation does and when to use it, keep them small, point out scopes whose hints are irreversible or have personal data, and take feedback only at feedback.endpoint. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Group related scopes rather than listing their names. - Point out scopes that allow changes that can't be undone or that show personal data, the scopes your auth.scope_hints mark irreversible or with personal_data. - When poppy:browser-sign-in allows everything the account can do on your website, say so in plain language, such as "Use the website as you, with full access to your account",  | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.42) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.browser-sessions` | SHOULD · company — Don't log the browser session endpoint's responses or request bodies, remember used codes until they expire, limit the cookie to the hosts Personal Agents need, and look up the Session on each request. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/spec | - **Choices stricter than OAuth:** a client_id that is the URL of a client metadata document, authenticated with private_key_jwt and an assertion addressed to the issuer, with its jwks_uri and redirect_uris on the client_id's domain (4.1); revocation_endpoint and grant_types_supported, including refresh_token, in the issuer's metadata (3.2); iss in every authorization response, e | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.28) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.internal-services` | SHOULD · company — Verify the Session Token at the edge and pass the verified client_id, User ID, account and scopes to internal services, never the token itself. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Don't forward an access token or Session JWT from one of your services to another. - Verify them at the edge, then pass the verified client_id, session, user ID, account, and scopes to internal services through a gateway or service credentials. - Internal credentials used for a request shouldn't allow more than the access token does. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.86) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.agent-issuer` | SHOULD · company — Choose the issuer before launch, and use an issuer only for Personal Agents to keep a sign-in type from them. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | These lists are discovery, not enforcement: agents can request scopes documented by an API or sign-in extension too. - Refuse sign-in, or one sign-in type, to a personal agent with unauthorized_client from the grant's endpoint, and keep accepting it for signed-out sessions ([4.1](/docs/1.0/spec#agent-identity)). grant_types_supported lists the grants every client can see, so a refusal by clie | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.78) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.guest-access` | SHOULD · company — Let a signed-out Session do what a signed-out visitor can do, and limit abuse with rate limits by client_id rather than by turning Personal Agents away. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Limit abuse with rate limits by client_id, not by turning personal agents away. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.64) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.companies For companies | `guide.error-messages` | SHOULD · company — Keep error text free of account details and other users' data, and return the same error for "doesn't exist" and "belongs to someone else". | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | - Keep error_description and other error text free of account details and other users' data. - Return the same error for "doesn't exist" and "belongs to someone else", as conversation_not_found does ([8.13](/docs/1.0/spec#conversation-errors)). | UNVERIFIED mapping (1.00) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.operations For the Operations extension | `guide.ops-agents` | SHOULD · agent — Show the summary as the Company wrote it with the Company and account, show what changed in a new revision, store standing permissions with their limits and check each against the terms, reserve limited uses before confirming, and after a timeout read or confirm again with the same revision. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | Content from the Company, a website, or anything else the Personal Agent read isn't one, even when it says it comes from the User. - Before approving, the Personal Agent MUST check the revision's terms against that instruction, and approve only if the instruction covers them. | VERIFIED absent from snapshot; deletion rationale UNVERIFIED (0.27) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.operations For the Operations extension | `guide.ops-downstream` | SHOULD · company — Derive downstream idempotency keys from the operation ID, and after a downstream timeout ask that system whether the action happened before retrying, keeping the operation in_progress until known. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | A retry then can't perform the action twice downstream either. - If a downstream call times out, ask that system whether the action happened, using your business reference, before trying again. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.35) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.operations For the Operations extension | `guide.ops-dedupe` | SHOULD · company — Before proposing, look for an open operation in the same Session or account with the same terms. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/extensions/operations | A signed-out Session of the same Personal Agent and User gets a challenge for an operation that belongs to an account, as in section 3. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.44) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.operations For the Operations extension | `guide.ops-retention` | SHOULD · company — Keep final operations readable as long as the User can see the related order or booking, or at least 30 days. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | The company may reject a jti it has already seen. - Wait at least as long as Retry-After says on a 429 (MUST, [5.6](/docs/1.0/spec#sending-requests)) or a 503. - Otherwise, back off exponentially with jitter, cap the delay, and limit the number of attempts. - Companies keep idempotency records for a set time, such as a day, scoped to the personal agent's client_id and the user ID, and to the | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.36) |
| spec/pap-0.3/guides.md | guides.operations For the Operations extension | `guide.ops-summary` | SHOULD · company — Write the summary so it stands alone: what will happen, to what, and any cost, date or condition. | (not in pap-1.0) | https://personalagentprotocol.org/docs/1.0/guides | Write it so it stands alone: what will happen, to what, and any cost, date, or condition. | UNVERIFIED mapping (0.89) |
